## §0 THE PREMISE

### 0.1 The owner directive, verbatim

**Owner's words (2026-08-05, in-chat; intent binding when architected):**

> "weighted rolls [should] operate a bit in memory. The things that were most
> successful for a circumstance or situation should likely be the thing that is most
> likely to be chosen more often. That is called habit, conditioning, and learning.
> The things that often failed in the situation should lose relative weight. They
> should go for anything that regard[s] decision-making for the actors. But countered
> to this these are things that also should be anticipated by enemies and allies in
> their own strategy as a counterforce. If settlement [A] knows that settlement B
> genuinely does action Z in a circumstance, this adds into the war of information
> and espionage on how settlement would react and whether settlement B knows
> settlement A's plans (which changes the situation and circumstance) and vice versa."

**Owner's scope emphasis (2026-08-06, verbatim):**

> "Learning shouldn't be limited just to war it should be for any decision fork in
> the program per actor per circumstance per action and on and on."

**Owner's volatility ruling (2026-08-06, verbatim):**

> "chaotic to lawful should influence that learning and memory. Chaotic makes both
> more volatile."

**Owner's cohesion ruling (2026-08-06, verbatim):**

> "trade … faith, strategy, route creation, route choice, and dynamics between
> settlements, goals, and goal orientation, etc. … make it cohesive fully"

### 0.2 The learning-as-state resolution — why replay AND explainability SURVIVE

The estate carried a standing law: **learning breaks replay and breaks
explainability.** That law is TRUE, and it is true of exactly one thing — **weight-
FUNCTION mutation**, where the formula itself changes as the world runs. A world
whose scoring functions rewrite themselves cannot be replayed from a seed (the code
that produced tick 400 no longer exists at tick 400 of the replay) and cannot be
explained (no authored sentence describes a formula nobody authored).

The owner's shape is not that. It is **LEARNING-AS-STATE**: the formulas stay frozen
and owner-signed; they gain ONE new **input** — `habitStrength(actor,
circumstanceClass, action)` — which is a persisted number derived deterministically
from the world's own graded outcomes. Three consequences, each an argument rather
than an assertion:

**(a) REPLAY SURVIVES, and the argument is a chain of four links.** ① The habit stock
is a pure function of the ORDERED SEQUENCE of graded closes that the world lived. ②
That sequence is itself a pure function of the seed, the code, and the DM's own acts
— it is the world's history, the thing the estate already replays. ③ The habit read
is taken from LAST-TICK state and written at NEXT-TICK apply (the `dispositionLedger`
timing law, quoted at §3b), so no chooser ever sees a half-updated mid-tick value. ④
The habit factor enters as a **MULTIPLICAND on a weight, never an exponent**, and
`stableSampleByWeight` consumes **exactly one** `rng.random()` call regardless of the
weights it is handed (MEASURED, `contestMath.js#stableSampleByWeight` — the body
takes one roll and walks the array), so a habit-tilted draw consumes the same stream
position and the same drawn value as an untilted one. Therefore: **same history ⇒
same habits ⇒ same choices ⇒ same history.** The loop closes on itself and is not
recursive across ticks. The one residual — floating-point decay across engines — is
fenced at §3b's ROUNDING FENCE and raised honestly as chair question Q3.

**(b) EXPLAINABILITY IMPROVES.** Today a chooser's receipt can say the score was
high. It cannot say WHY this court, specifically, leans this way. Habit makes that
sayable: it is a NAMED REASON FAMILY over a CLOSED circumstance vocabulary and a
CLOSED action vocabulary, so every tilt has an authored sentence
("as this court reliably does when its granaries run thin"). The estate gains
explanation; it does not spend any.

**(c) THE DOSSIER GAINS A PRODUCT.** The doctrine sheet — twelve lines at most,
one per circumstance class — is a legible artifact a DM can read, a spy can steal,
and a liar can forge. It is the first thing in this estate that is simultaneously a
game-mechanical input, a narrative object, and an intelligence commodity.

**THE FIVE PLACES THE WIDER DIRECTIVE OVERRODE A CENSUS RECOMMENDATION** (recorded so
a reader of the censuses is not misled):

1. Census 1 recommended demographics as wave one and treated the war chooser as "the
   anchor but not the opener". The owner's TOTAL SCOPE makes the question moot: every
   fork joins or is enumerated as deferred-with-reason. Ordering survives as
   engineering; scope does not.
2. Census 1 recommended ruling migration and the NPC grain out of scope. Refinement 8
   supersedes: per-NPC habits attach **only where per-NPC decision machinery already
   exists** — which is a rule, not an exclusion. Migration stays out, but by
   refinement 17's explicit STAY call with a stated reason, never by silence.
3. Census 2 recommended `SEVERITY_LADDER` as a candidate if more than win/loss is
   wanted. Refinement 12 makes it mandatory: grades come ONLY from existing outcome
   vocabularies and the severity ladder SCALES the update.
4. Census 4 recorded the war-chooser wiring as PARKED/owner-gated. **REFUTED BY EVENT
   — see R1.**
5. No census contemplated law-band modulation. Refinement 16 adds it, and it lands
   with zero new machinery because `lawWordFor` and `LAW_WORDS` are already minted
   (`src/domain/worldPulse/lawWord.js` — MEASURED at HEAD:
   `LAW_WORDS = ['balanced','lawful','lawless']`).

### 0.3 The refutation register — R1-R18 (nothing builds on the left column)

**R1 — "The war-chooser wiring is PARKED and owner-gated." REFUTED BY EVENT.**
Census 4 read the docs correctly (`FABLE_VALIDATION_QUEUE.md` still carries
"deferred to a future owner ruling per CR-ES-4") and drew a conclusion the owner had
already overtaken. The **blanket queue sign-off (owner, 2026-08-05, verbatim: "I give
permission for everything that requires my sign off in the full queue")** names it
explicitly in the RELEASED-TO-BUILD list: *"The war-chooser spy-before-decision wiring
(CR-ES-4's deferred arm) — SIGNED; queue after the ES spine as its own wave."*
CONSEQUENCE: census 4's recommended action (i)/(ii) — asking the owner for a combined
ruling — is DISCHARGED. What remains is engineering, and it is this volume's §4 HB-5:
**the habit chooser read and the signed spy-before-decision wiring land JOINTLY,
because they retrofit the same frozen surface and a second visit to `settlementStrategy`
costs a second size-ratchet negotiation.** ⚠ The queue documents still read
"deferred"; the fold that records the grant is queue task #53, PENDING. This volume's
§5 carries the correcting clause so the two never disagree in the tree.

**R2 — "MISSION_GRADES is the credit-at-close precedent to inherit." SHAPE ONLY.**
Census 2 measured it: `missionGradeFor({ bestConfidence01, demand })` grades a RESULT
against a BAR. It takes no mission id, no opening action, no circumstance, and
`src/domain/certification/subsystemRowsVirtual.js` calls the espionage subsystem "A
SUBSYSTEM WITH A GATE AND NO CALLER" verbatim. It is a **vocabulary-shape** precedent
— a closed grade set plus a separate rank map — and **not a working credit-assignment
join.** The directive's refinement 4 is honored by copying the SHAPE; the JOIN is
built here (§3a, the pledge book). No wave brief may say "as MISSION_GRADES does"
about join behaviour.

**R3 — "Habits ride the existing decay law." TRUE, BUT THE FAMILY HAS NO RIDERS.**
MEASURED: `grep -rn "from '.*bandedStock.js'" src/` returns ZERO hits.
`decayTowardNeutral` is consumed only by tests. Meanwhile `dispositionLedger`
hand-rolls `Math.pow(0.5, age / CHANNEL_HALF_LIFE_TICKS)` with
`CHANNEL_HALF_LIFE_TICKS = 1040`, and `bandedStock`'s own header names FIFTEEN such
forks. So "never a second decay law" was already violated fifteen ways before habit
existed. RULING J-HB-3 (§6): **the habit family becomes `decayTowardNeutral`'s FIRST
RIDER**, and the sixteenth-fork temptation is refused in writing. ⚠ THE UNITS TRAP:
`dispositionLedger`'s 1040 is TICKS; `bandedStock`'s `a_generation` is 1040 WEEKS.
The numbers coincide only because a tick is a week on the default interval. Habit
converts tick-age to WEEK-age through the same `INTERVAL_WEEKS` contract
`bandedStock` derives its half-lives from — VERIFY-AT-BUILD the exact conversion
symbol, and STOP if it does not exist rather than assuming 1:1.

**R4 — "The occupation/trade/coup records need opening stamps." AVOIDED, NOT OWED.**
Census 2 (H-3) and census 4 correctly measured that three of six major lifecycle
records carry no reference to the decision that opened them, and concluded the habit
wave owes a persisted-shape change on foreign records — an owner-gated schema move.
**THE PLEDGE BOOK MAKES IT UNNECESSARY** (§3a). The habit ledger keeps its OWN
opening record, keyed by an episode key the open mints. Nothing foreign changes shape.
⛔⛔ **THE SENTENCE THAT STOOD HERE IS STRUCK AT SOURCE — SEE J-HB-13.** It read: *"the
close must be able to recompute the same episode key the open computed … a read
obligation, not a schema obligation, and it is satisfied at all seven joined closes."*
**BOTH HALVES FELL.** The recompute-at-both-ends claim was REFUTED at close 1 — the
chooser runs a tick before the deployment exists and the close's delta carries
`{id, outcome, magnitude}` only, so neither side can compute the key — and J-HB-13 ruled
that **the episode key is a VALUE MINTED AT OPEN AND CARRIED**, which makes it a
persistence obligation at two records (`deployment.habitEpisode` and
`treaty.habitEpisode`, Q1) rather than a read obligation at none. **AND THE DENOMINATOR
WAS STALE TOO: the closes are EIGHT, not seven** — close 8 (the NPC goal) was enumerated
at the polish (R18) and this sentence was never re-parsed against it. ⚠ **R4's SURVIVING
CLAIM, which is what the row is actually for, is unaffected:** the pledge book still
means **NO FOREIGN RECORD CHANGES SHAPE TO CARRY AN OPENING STAMP** — the two carried
fields are additive drop-when-absent fields on records the estate already persists, not
the occupation/tradeWar/coup schema moves census 2 (H-3) thought were owed.

**R5 — "The chartered prefix set is nine (SC-7)." STALE.** The MEMORY index says
nine. MEASURED at HEAD, `tests/domain/couplingRegistry.test.js`:
`CHARTERED_VOLUME_PREFIXES = Object.freeze(['WR','TR','GR','WF','POP','IN','INT','SP','CW','ES','WY'])`
— **ELEVEN.** `HB` is the TWELFTH, and because `COUPLING_ID_SHAPE` is BUILT from that
array, the first `CPL-<n>.<DIR>.HB-<n>.<facet>` row REDS until the prefix joins. One
array member, one new leaf, same commit.

**R6 — "`convergence.reactiveResponse` is structurally `habitStrength(actor, class,
action)` already, minus the learning." REFUTED, AND THE SEAM IS STRUCK.** MEASURED at
HEAD: `export function reactiveResponse({ believed = null, relation =
REACTIVE_RELATIONS.ENEMY_COLUMN, myStrength01 = 0.5, exhaustion01 = 0 } = {})` — **no
`worldState`, no actor id**, JSDoc closing "Pure, deterministic." The body is a TOTAL
CONSTANT DISPATCH on `relation`: absent belief ⇒ `move: 'stand'`; `ALLY_UNDER_SIEGE` ⇒
`'reinforce'`; `ENEMY_INTERVENER` ⇒ `'counter_intervene'`; else `'intercept'`.
`REACTIVE_RELATIONS` has exactly THREE members, so relation→move is total and constant
and **`ev` is computed but never used to choose.** `grep -rn "reactiveResponse" src/
tests/` returns the declaration plus FIVE hits, all in
`tests/domain/convergence.test.js` — **ZERO callers in `src`.** CONSEQUENCE: it is not a
fork, it is not reachable, and a habit or anticipation term there would either multiply
an unused number or require converting a constant dispatch into a weighted fork —
which J-HB-6 forbids elsewhere and which this volume never priced here. **RULED
(J-HB-16): `reactiveResponse` takes a DEAD-CODE disposition row in the fork registry
and a chair-owed report. IT IS NOT DELETED BY THIS PROGRAM** — an uncalled export with
five live tests is somebody's declared deferral until a reading of `convergence.js`'s
own charter says otherwise, and deleting foreign dead code inside a habit wave is
exactly the scope creep the wave discipline exists to stop. **Site 11 and anticipation
seam 4 are struck**; the anticipation seam they held is replaced by the WAR CHOOSER
(§3d), which R1's grant and A2 now release.

**R7 — "`settlementPolitics.blocDecisionFactor`'s `'fortify'` test is A DEAD ARM;
delete the arm." REFUTED IN SUBSTANCE — THE DELETE RULING IS RETRACTED.** MEASURED:
`settlementPolitics.js` — `} else if (move === 'fortify' || move === 'defend') { pull =
(end === 'survival' ? 0.6 : end === 'seats' ? 0.2 : 0); }`. **The ARM is a DISJUNCTION
and its second disjunct is `defend`, which `enumerateMoves` emits UNCONDITIONALLY**
(`settlementStrategy.js`, under the in-source comment "defend — always legal. Strong
when besieged or when the settlement is weak.", `scored.defend = clamp01(...)` outside
every conditional), and `blocDecisionFactor` is wired live at
`settlementStrategy.js` — `? { factorFor: (move) => blocDecisionFactor(worldState,
String(sId), item, move) }`. **The arm fires for every settlement with a ruling bloc.**
Only the `move === 'fortify' ||` DISJUNCT is dead. ⚠ `'fortify'` is NOT an orphan
token estate-wide either: `mobilizationReactions.js` emits a `'fortify'` move in its own
vocabulary — so the defect is a CROSS-VOCABULARY COLLISION (a politics factor reading a
strategy move key that only the mobilization vocabulary spells), not a phantom.
**CORRECTED RULING (§4, HB-1): delete the `move === 'fortify' ||` DISJUNCT ALONE, and
correct the proof** — the behaviour-identity argument is NOT "an arm that never fired
cannot change an output" (false: the arm fires) but "**for every move
`enumerateMoves` emits, the disjunction's truth value is unchanged by removing a
disjunct that no emitted move satisfies**", which the same-seed goldens then confirm
rather than assume. ⛔ An implementer who deletes the whole `else if` deletes live
behaviour and will read the resulting golden red as an error somewhere else.

**R8 — "`proseNumerics` is RED AT BASE (401 vs 410), awaiting a RULED re-record."
DISCHARGED AT HEAD.** MEASURED: `tests/lint/proseNumerics.test.js` carries
`// CR-FP-2 — THE ONE RULED RE-RECORD (FP cycle 1 close). 404 -> 413.` and
`const REVIEWED_TOTAL_CEILING = 413;`; executed,
`python3 -c "import json; print(len(json.load(open('tests/lint/.prose-numerics-baseline.json'))))"`
→ **413**. Neither 401 nor 410 is the live number and the chair STOP is closed. **§3g's
warning and J-HB-8's veto cost are re-grounded on 413.** The narrowing J-HB-8 makes
(rungs, never counts) survives on its own merits — the stock is a decayed continuous
value whose "count" is not an integer anyone can honestly print — but it no longer
leans on a red that does not exist. ⚠ The live discipline that DOES bind: the scanner is
an exact-equality ceiling in BOTH directions, so an HB wave that adds prose must
**re-record its own delta against 413 with an attribution**, never inherit and never
raise.

**R9 — "`HABIT_HOLD_BANDS` borrows `CHANNEL_BANDS`; a walker imports BOTH sides and
asserts verbatim equality in BOTH directions; ⚠ the array is codepoint-sorted."
REFUTED THREE WAYS.** MEASURED, `dispositionLedger.js`: `const CHANNEL_BANDS =
Object.freeze([ {maxExclusive: 0.2, name: 'restrained'}, {maxExclusive: 0.4, name:
'measured'}, {maxExclusive: 0.6, name: 'settled'}, {maxExclusive: 0.8, name: 'marked'},
{maxExclusive: Infinity, name: 'dominant'} ]);`. (i) It is **`const`, not `export
const`** — there is no both-sides import to make. (ii) It is an array of **OBJECTS
ORDERED ASCENDING BY THRESHOLD**, consumed as `CHANNEL_BANDS.find((band) => stock <
band.maxExclusive)?.name`, which only works because the order IS the rank — **the index
IS the rank, the exact opposite of the warning**, and the rung words
restrained/measured/settled/marked/dominant are not codepoint-sorted (d<m<m<r<s).
(iii) Its only outward path is `BANDS: CHANNEL_BANDS` inside the exported
`DISPOSITION_CHANNEL_TUNING`. **RESPELLED (§3d): the borrow is ONE-DIRECTIONAL.** HB
imports `DISPOSITION_CHANNEL_TUNING` and DERIVES its rungs by projection; one walker
asserts the import is verbatim and that the projection is threshold-ascending. There is
no second array to keep equal to a first.

**R10 — "Habit converts tick-age to WEEK-age through the `INTERVAL_WEEKS` contract —
VERIFY-AT-BUILD the exact conversion symbol, and STOP if it does not exist." THE STOP
IS DISCHARGED WITHOUT A CONVERTER, BECAUSE THE WORLD ALREADY CARRIES WEEKS.** The
critic is right that no tick→week converter exists and that none could exist for a
bare `[s, t]` pair. It is also unnecessary. MEASURED: `worldState.js` states the
temporal constitution in-source — *"INTEGER WEEKS ARE CANONICAL … the advance path
stores/advances integer elapsed weeks and DERIVES every label from them"* — and the
authoritative scalar is **`worldState.calendar.elapsedWeeks`**, seeded `elapsedWeeks: 0`
at `createDefaultWorldState`, read tolerantly by `weeksFromCalendar` (new-format
`elapsedWeeks`, legacy `elapsedMonths × 4`, "NO save migration — old shapes read
losslessly forever"), and consumed today by `pulseKernel`, `generosityKernel`,
`npcLadderKernel`, `mapDress`. **RULED (J-HB-17): THE HABIT LEDGER STAMPS WEEKS, NEVER
TICKS.** The stock pair becomes `[s, w]` with `w = calendar.elapsedWeeks` at write, and
`ageWeeks = nowWeeks - w` is a subtraction of two integers off the campaign clock —
handed straight to `decayTowardNeutral(value, neutral, ageWeeks, band)`, whose signature
MEASURED demands weeks (`@param {number} ageWeeks weeks elapsed since value was
written`). No converter, no interval history, no sixteenth fork. §1.1, §3b and §8.5 are
rewritten to the week stamp, and the pledge book's age sweep becomes
`PLEDGE_MAX_AGE_WEEKS` on the same clock.

**R13 — "Site 14 `espionageMath.deliberationRead` joins as a cadence fork (HB-9)."

**R16 — "The tilt-never-lock pin bounds the SELECTION-PROBABILITY RATIO inside

CONSTRUCTION." REFUTED MATHEMATICALLY — THE PIN IS UNSATISFIABLE AND THE
'BY CONSTRUCTION' ARGUMENT IS FALSE.** The convex-mean fact the amendment cited is true;
the conclusion does not follow. Under `p'_i = p_i·f_i / Σ p_j·f_j` the per-probability
ratio is `f_i / mean_f`, and because `mean_f` ranges over `[min f, max f]` the ratio
ranges over `[(1−s)/(1+s), (1+s)/(1−s)]` = `[0.4815, 2.0769]` at `s = 0.35` — **not
`(0.65, 1.35)`.** EXECUTED at HEAD-measured constants (`STRATEGY_K = 3.5`,
`HABIT_SPAN = 0.35`): a randomized sweep over 200,000 reachable `(score, factor)` states
with `n ∈ {2,3,4,5,11}` violates the claimed bound at rate **0.2643 / 0.2645** (two
independent re-executions; ⛔ the polish's **0.1756** is STRUCK — it does not reproduce,
and a rate is a sampling-distribution artefact, not a property of the law) with a worst
observed per-probability ratio of **1.9380**. **RULED (P1, J-HB-18): the per-probability
claim is DELETED AS FALSE and the invariant is RESPELLED ON PAIRWISE ODDS RATIOS**, which
IS true by construction because the factor ratio survives normalization untouched. The
full statement, the by-construction argument on factor ratios, and the anchor-site
hand-walk are at §3b. ⚠ **THE DANGER THIS CLOSES is not a wrong sentence — it is a wave
chartered to land a RED pin as its own law's proof.** HB-0 and HB-5 were both chartered
to build it.

### 0.4 The self-balancing loop as DESIGN LAW

The directive does not merely permit the counterforce; it makes it the regulator. The
law, stated once and binding on every wave:

> **A habit that becomes KNOWN becomes EXPLOITABLE. The information war is the
> variance regulator, and it is the ONLY variance regulator this design is permitted
> to add.**

Three mechanisms discharge it, and no fourth is authorized:

1. **TILT, NEVER LOCK** (§3b). The stock is capped and floored by a
   toward-the-bound update form that cannot ATTAIN either bound, so no option is ever
   driven to zero. The dead-arm law survives by construction, not by clamping.
2. **ANTICIPATION PUNISHES OVER-CONDITIONING** (§3d). An observer that has learned a
   subject's doctrine tilts its own counter-choice against it. A perfectly
   conditioned actor is a perfectly predicted actor.
3. **THE DEVIATION DAMPER** (§3f). An actor that is AWARE it is watched follows its
   own habit LESS. This is the owner's "deviate-from-pattern candidate gains weight",
   expressed as relative weight — it adds **no additional move** to any vocabulary, mints
   no new option, and reads only signals that already exist.

⚠ **THE LOOP IS NOT A TUNING KNOB.** Variance is regulated by the information war or
it is not regulated. A future wave that proposes a "variance floor" constant, a
random-deviation roll, or an exploration term is proposing a FOURTH regulator and is
a STOP-and-report, not a tuning row.

### 0.5 What is genuinely new, and what is wiring

**The measured verdict: this layer is roughly half wiring and half mint** — a
different balance from the espionage program, and the difference is the pledge book.

**Already shipped, consumed verbatim, never re-spelled:**
the decay law (`bandedStock.decayTowardNeutral` + `HALF_LIFE_BANDS`) · the severity
ladder (`bandFamilies.SEVERITY_LADDER`, purpose-built and consumer-less) · the law
word (`lawWord.lawWordFor` + `LAW_WORDS`) · the learned-stock prototype in every
respect but the circumstance key (`dispositionLedger` — per-actor stocks, closed
source vocabulary, learn rates, decay-toward-neutral, capped tilt returning EXACTLY
1.0 when absent, banded prose projection, read-last/write-next timing, default-off
flag) · the credit-at-close reference implementation (`demographicsPlans` — a
persisted plan stamped at open with the circumstance BAND, the winning action, and
its reasons, closed by a six-word outcome vocabulary) · the belief-axis extension
recipe (SP-B's five joins) · the tap ladder and the plant road (ES) · the dossier
projection (`settlementBeliefs` + `BeliefDivergenceBand`) · the sub-ledger home and
its accessors (`distanceRead.{has,get,set,drop}SpatialLedger`).

**Exactly five things are genuinely new:**

1. **THE CIRCUMSTANCE KEY.** `dispositionLedger` is keyed (actor → channel). Habit is
   keyed (actor → circumstanceClass → action). That one extra dimension, plus a closed
   typed vocabulary for it, is the whole conceptual delta.
2. **THE PLEDGE BOOK** — the credit-assignment join the estate has never had. Every
   graded close in the tree today names the ACTOR, never the ACTION that opened it
   (census 2 H-4, CONFIRMED). §3a builds the join without touching any foreign record.
3. **THE ACTION VOCABULARY FOR THE WAR CHOOSER.** `settlementStrategy`'s moves exist
   only as branch keys, objective-bag keys, and a hand-maintained local `Set` — and
   the drift is already REAL, though **not in the shape the compile claimed (R7)**:
   `settlementPolitics.blocDecisionFactor` tests `move === 'fortify' || move ===
   'defend'`, and `'fortify'` is a token only the MOBILIZATION vocabulary
   (`mobilizationReactions.js`) spells. **The live arm fires on `defend`; the DISJUNCT
   is the dead thing, and the defect is a cross-vocabulary collision** — precisely the
   class a frozen exported totality prevents. HB-1 mints `STRATEGY_MOVES`, retires the
   local `Set`, deletes the dead DISJUNCT alone, and lands the walker that reds any
   module branching on a strategy-move token outside the frozen export.
4. **THE BELIEVED-DOCTRINE AXIS** — a fourth SP-B subject family, `doctrineBands`.
5. **THE CHOOSER-TOTALITY REGISTRY AND ITS WALKER** — the structural enforcement of
   the owner's TOTAL SCOPE, so no future decision fork joins the tree unclassified.

**JUDGMENT J-HB-1 (the host).** Habit state lives in **ONE conditionally-materialized
`spatialLedgers` sub-key with exactly ONE writer** — the L4 sanctioned growth
surface — and NOT as an extension of `dispositionLedger`. Reasons, in order: (a)
`dispositionStats` is materialized only under `dispositionChannelsEnabled` and is read
by SIXTEEN modules; adding a dimension to it would put every habit read behind a
foreign flag and every disposition read at risk from a habit bug; (b) the two have
different KEYS (channel vs class×action), different SOURCES (13 outcome kinds vs
seven graded closes), different DECAY RATIONALES (appetite vs memory), and different
GRAINS (four channels vs twelve classes); (c) `dispositionLedger` is itself one of
`bandedStock`'s fifteen forks, so extending it would make the habit stock a sixteenth
fork by inheritance — the exact thing refinement 1 forbids. THE COST OF THE VETO:
folding habit into `dispositionStats` saves one sub-ledger row in `spatialUsage.js`
and buys a permanent flag entanglement. Say "veto" to co-host; the price is that
`habitConditioningEnabled` becomes unusable without `dispositionChannelsEnabled`.

**JUDGMENT J-HB-2 (the deciding identity).** Habits attach to the **SETTLEMENT-
INSTITUTION** for settlement-grade decisions (refinement 8), carrying a SEAT STAMP so
that a change of ruling seat applies a one-time decay shock rather than a reset or a
carry-forward. Per-NPC habits attach **only where per-NPC decision machinery already
exists** — measured today as exactly two families: the espionage/errand person
choices and the NPC ladder's goal machinery (`npcLadderGoals`, the estate's ONLY
existing open→graded-close loop keyed to an individual actor AND an action). Every
other NPC-grain fork is a STAY or DEFER row with a written reason. This is the
ledger-explosion fence: the actor denominator is settlements plus a bounded, named
NPC set, never the roster.

---

## §1 THE CANONICAL MODEL — one conditional sub-key, one writer, zero new top-level keys

### 1.1 The habit ledger

```
worldState.spatialLedgers.habits           // HABIT_LEDGER_KEY = 'habits'
                                           // conditionally materialized; ABSENT when
                                           // dark or empty (drop-when-empty at EVERY
                                           // level — L4/T4: absent, never null)
= {
  v: 1,                                    // sub-ledger schema version (SEP from
                                           // WORLD_STATE_SCHEMA_VERSION — no world
                                           // migration is owed; see §1.5)

  stocks: {                                // THE LEARNED STATE
    <actorId>: {
      seat: string,                        // the deciding identity's seat stamp
                                           // (refinement 8); drop-when-unresolvable
      byClass: {
        <circumstanceClass>: {             // a member of CIRCUMSTANCE_CLASSES (12)
          <actionToken>: [s, w]            // s = stock, INTEGER 0..10000 in 1e-4
                                           // units (THE ROUNDING FENCE, §3b);
                                           // w = worldState.calendar.elapsedWeeks at
                                           // the write (⭐ WEEKS, NEVER TICKS — R10 /
                                           // J-HB-17; the decay law demands weeks and
                                           // the campaign clock already carries them)
        }
      }
    }
  },

  open: {                                  // THE PLEDGE BOOK — the credit join (§3a)
    <pledgeId>: [a, c, k, w]               // actorId, circumstanceClass, actionToken,
                                           // openedWeek (the SAME clock). FIRST-WINS
                                           // per pledgeId.
  }
}
```

⭐⭐ **THE CLOCK CONTRACT, STATED AS A SEAM (R10 / J-HB-17 / §8.1 row 13) — this is the
one place the amendment changed the persisted shape.** The habit family reads exactly
one time symbol and it is **`worldState.calendar.elapsedWeeks`**: an integer, seeded 0
at `createDefaultWorldState`, advanced by the advance path, and read tolerantly by
`weeksFromCalendar` (which recovers `elapsedMonths × 4` for legacy saves and states
in-source "NO save migration — old shapes read losslessly forever"). Habit therefore
does NOT convert ticks to weeks, does NOT read `INTERVAL_WEEKS` directly, and does NOT
depend on any per-advance interval history — **the three things the compile's
VERIFY-AT-BUILD clause was worried about, all dissolved by stamping the clock the world
already keeps.** ⚠ THE ONE OBLIGATION THIS CREATES, pinned at HB-2: a habit write that
cannot resolve `calendar.elapsedWeeks` **writes nothing at all** rather than stamping a
0 that would read as "written at the dawn of the world" and decay a fresh stock to
neutral on its first read. Absent clock ⇒ absent row ⇒ factor exactly 1 — the same
neutral-identity door everything else goes through.

**Why an array pair and not an object per row.** A habit ledger is the widest
per-actor structure this estate has proposed, and it rides the cloud JSONB payload on
every sync (§8's lifecycle row 6). `[s, w]` costs ~14 bytes where
`{"stock01":0.6234,"lastWeek":412}` costs ~34. ⭐ **RECONCILED AT THE POLISH (P10) — the
compile printed ~90KB and ~220KB, which match no bound this volume states.** §1.4's
measured bound is 48 rows per actor across 180 settlements, and the arithmetic is
`180 × 48 × 14 = 120,960 B ≈ 121KB` against `180 × 48 × 34 = 293,760 B ≈ 294KB`. **So
the honest sentence is: the difference between a ~121KB and a ~294KB addition to a large
campaign** — §1.4 stated its own half correctly and §1.1 did not, and a chair pricing the
legibility trade was being shown two wrong numbers. The RULING is unaffected (the pair is
still ~2.4× cheaper, and 2.43× is the exact ratio 34/14), which is precisely why the
error could sit unnoticed: **an arithmetic slip that does not move a conclusion is the
kind this estate catches only by executing it.** The pair is
positional and therefore ILLEGIBLE, so it is validated by an exact-arity normalizer
and never read by hand — the `[weight, tieKey]` idiom the contest family already uses.
⚠ The positional shape is a real legibility cost and it is taken deliberately; the
cure is the normalizer plus the dossier projection (§3e), which is where a human
reads a habit.

**Drop-when-neutral, at four levels.** ① A stock whose integer value equals
`NEUTRAL_I` (5000) after a write is DROPPED, not stored. ② A class with no surviving
actions is dropped. ③ An actor with no surviving classes and no seat-relevant state is
dropped. ④ When `stocks` and `open` are both empty the whole sub-key is dropped via
`dropSpatialLedger`, which — MEASURED, `distanceRead.js` — drops the entire
`spatialLedgers` namespace when it was the last sub-ledger, "so an emptied world stays
byte-identical to a dormant one". **This is refinement 10 (COLD-START = DARK-STATE =
NEUTRAL) discharged STRUCTURALLY rather than by a guard:** an empty ledger is not a
ledger of zeros, it is an absent key, and an absent key returns a factor of exactly
1.0 through the same door a dark flag does.

### 1.2 The closed CIRCUMSTANCE-CLASS vocabulary — TWELVE, total, precedence-ordered

Minted in `src/domain/worldPulse/habit/habitVocabulary.js`. **THE BORROW CENSUS
(J-WR-10-B — a mint must record what it looked for), executed at HEAD:** all twelve
tokens were measured against `src/` and ELEVEN returned ZERO hits. The twelfth
candidate, `ordinary`, returned SEVEN and was REJECTED in favour of `unpressed` (zero
hits). The class NAMES are minted; every class DEFINITION borrows an existing typed
read and mints no second spelling of any band.

| # | Class | Fires when | Borrowed from |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | `siege_pressed` | own or vassal settlement under siege | `STRESSOR_CATALOG` `'siege'` |
| 2 | `war_open` | a live war names this actor as a belligerent | `WAR_STRESSOR_TYPES` `'wartime'` |
| 3 | `terms_offered` | a peace / pact answer is due from this actor | `warPeaceDecision` + GR-2's `answerDueTick` |
| 4 | `coalition_called` | a ratification ballot or coalition obligation is live | `RATIFICATION_VERDICTS` / the coalition episode |
| 5 | `faith_contested` | schism, conversion pressure, or a tradition claim is live | `religious_authority` band + `DRAMA_CLASS_REGISTRY` `'schism_contest'` |
| 6 | `legitimacy_shaken` | `public_legitimacy` OR `ruling_authority` is strained-or-worse | `CAUSAL_BANDS` |
| 7 | `stores_thin` | `food_security` OR `economic_capacity` is strained-or-worse | `CAUSAL_BANDS` |
| 8 | `crowding_pressed` | the demographic overflow band is `pressed` or `overflowing` | `OVERFLOW_BANDS` |
| 9 | `trade_contested` | a supply prize this actor buys or sells is contested | `tradeWarState` |
| 10 | `route_menaced` | a route this actor depends on carries live embattlement or toll pressure | `embattlementLevel` |
| 11 | `rival_ascendant` | a hostile-class counterpart's BELIEVED strength band is ahead | `PRIMARY_RELATIONSHIP_TYPES` + the believed balance ladder |
| 12 | `unpressed` | none of 1-11 fires | — the TOTAL fallback |

**THREE LAWS ON THE VOCABULARY, each walker-enforced:**

**(a) TOTALITY BY CONSTRUCTION.** `unpressed` guarantees the classifier is total:
`circumstanceClassOf(actor, worldState)` ALWAYS returns exactly one member. There is
no null arm, no "unknown" arm, and no multi-class return. A decision that could not be
classified is a bug, not a mystery — the `PLAN_OUTCOMES` doctrine, borrowed.

**(b) PRECEDENCE IS DECLARED, NEVER DERIVED.** `CIRCUMSTANCE_PRECEDENCE` is a frozen
ordered array, most-acute first, exactly as in the table above. The classifier walks
it and returns the first match. ⚠ The `CIRCUMSTANCE_CLASSES` export is CODEPOINT-
SORTED for totality (the house idiom); the PRECEDENCE array is semantic. **Reading
the sorted array's index as a precedence rank inverts the meaning** — the exact trap
`espionageMath.js` names in-source about `MISSION_GRADES` vs `MISSION_GRADE_ORDER`
(hazard H-10). The walker asserts the two arrays are permutations of each other and
that no module but the vocabulary leaf spells a class token.

**(c) NO CROSS-CLASS BLEED IN v1, RECORDED AS AN OWNER FUTURE-WIDENING (refinement
11).** Classes are independent in v1. A stock learned under `siege_pressed` contributes
NOTHING to a decision taken under `war_open`, even though the two are adjacent — and the
adjacency is exactly why the rule is written down rather than left to taste. **Bleed is
an OWNER FUTURE-WIDENING, on the books at chair question Q5, never a tuning knob**, and
the walker pins that no read composes two class keys. ⛔ **The one thing forbidden now,
in writing: bleed must never arrive as a constant.** A bleed coefficient is a
credit-assignment claim wearing a dial's clothing — it says "this outcome partly graded
that decision", which is the creep refinement 4 exists to hold. When it arrives it
arrives as a structural change with its own wave and its own owner signature.

**GROWTH IS OWNER-SIGNED, AND THE CEILING IS PINNED AS A NUMBER (refinement 9).**
Twelve is a **CEILING**, not a target: the directive's words are "initial set ≤ 12
classes; growth by owner-signed addition only". The tripwire is therefore TWO
assertions, not one — `expect(CIRCUMSTANCE_CLASSES.length).toBe(12)` **and**
`expect(CIRCUMSTANCE_CLASSES.length).toBeLessThanOrEqual(HABIT_CLASS_CEILING)` with
`HABIT_CLASS_CEILING = 12` exported from the vocabulary leaf and named in the failure
message as OWNER-SIGNED. ⚠ The reason both are owed: an exact-count pin alone reads as
a re-record chore and gets bumped by whoever adds the thirteenth class; a pin that
names the CEILING and the SIGNATURE tells that person they are standing in front of an
owner gate. The count pin catches drift; the ceiling pin refuses it.

### 1.3 The action vocabularies — REQUIRED, never minted by this family

The habit key's third dimension is a token from **the deciding site's OWN frozen
action vocabulary.** The habit family NEVER mints an action word. A site whose action
set is not a frozen exported totality **cannot join the learning system**, and minting
that totality is that site's own wave slice.

MEASURED at HEAD, the joined sites' action vocabularies split three ways:

- **ALREADY FROZEN AND EXPORTED (consume verbatim):** `ENGAGEMENT_MOVES` (4) ·
  `REACTIVE_MOVES` (4) · `RESPONSES` (6, demographics) · `VERDICTS` (4, generosity) ·
  `BALLOT_DECISIONS` (2) · `CAPTOR_CHOICES` (3) · `HOME_ANSWERS` (3) ·
  `INSTRUMENT_KEYS` · `ARMY_ENVOY_PRIVATE_GOALS` (3) · `ARMY_INTERCEPTION_DECISIONS`
  (2) · `DELIBERATION_VERDICTS` (3) · `CONQUEST_INTENTS` (4).
- **MUST BE MINTED (HB-1's charter):** the war chooser's moves. `{defend, hold,
  deploy, sue_for_peace}` exist as branch keys in `enumerateMoves`; the seven levers
  `{reroute, embargo, credit, missionize, legitimacy, prestige, opportunity}` exist as
  keys of the objective `levers` bag; and a THIRD hand-maintained copy lives in a local
  `martialMoves` Set. **The drift is already real and already shipped — and R7 corrects
  its shape:** `settlementPolitics.blocDecisionFactor` tests `move === 'fortify' ||
  move === 'defend'`. The arm is LIVE (through `defend`, which `enumerateMoves` emits
  unconditionally); the `'fortify'` DISJUNCT is dead, and `'fortify'` is a token from a
  DIFFERENT vocabulary — `mobilizationReactions.js`'s own move set. **A politics factor
  reading a strategy-move key that only the mobilization vocabulary spells is the
  cross-vocabulary collision that a frozen exported totality exists to make
  impossible.** HB-1 mints `STRATEGY_MOVES` in exactly the shape `convergence.js`
  already uses for `ENGAGEMENT_MOVES`, retires the local Set, deletes the dead DISJUNCT
  alone, and lands the walker that reds any module branching on a strategy-move token
  outside the frozen export — the structural cure, of which the deletion is only the
  first instance.
- **OPEN BY NATURE, THEREFORE NEVER A HABIT AXIS:** target ids, supplier ids, buyer ×
  asset pairs, destination ids, route paths. **RULING J-HB-4: habits key on TYPED
  CIRCUMSTANCE CLASSES and TYPED ACTIONS only, never on counterpart identity.** Three
  reasons: an id-keyed ledger grows without bound per actor; a closed prose vocabulary
  cannot name it; and a doctrine sheet keyed on ids is not stealable in any meaningful
  sense — it is a database, not a sheet. Where a site's real choice is "which
  counterpart", the habit axis is the site's typed POSTURE (whether to contest at all,
  whether to escalate), and the identity choice stays exactly as it is today.

### 1.4 The bound, measured and enforced

The habit ledger is the one place in this program where cost is a real design input,
because it rides the cloud JSONB payload on every sync (§8, lifecycle path 6). The
bound is enforced three ways, all deterministic:

- **CLASSES ≤ 12** by the closed vocabulary.
- **ACTIONS PER CLASS ≤ the site vocabulary's own size** — the largest joined
  vocabulary is `STRATEGY_MOVES` at eleven, so ≤ 11.
- **ROWS PER ACTOR ≤ `HABIT_ROWS_PER_ACTOR_CAP`** (proposed 48, raw-authored). When a
  write would exceed the cap, the row NEAREST NEUTRAL is evicted, ties broken by
  codepoint on `<class>.<action>`. **Deterministic eviction is load-bearing** — a cap
  enforced by insertion order would make the ledger depend on iteration order and
  break replay.

Worked bound: 48 rows × ~14 bytes ≈ 670 bytes per actor; a 180-settlement campaign
with every settlement at the cap ≈ 120KB. In practice drop-when-neutral keeps live
rows far below the cap. **THE OBLIGATION: HB-2 MEASURES the real distribution on a
generated corpus and records the measured median and p95 in the module header** — an
unmeasured bound is a promise, and this estate does not ship promises as fences.

### 1.5 The Lifecycle-paths clause (L4 — written before any writer builds)

The FOURTEEN inherited paths are the EP volume's measured set (§8.4 of the epoch draft),
and they bind here verbatim; **this volume adds a FIFTEENTH of its own —
`preview / forecast` — so the habit ledger answers on FIFTEEN.** This clause states the
habit ledger's answer on each; §8.5 carries the full table with the writers named.

- **create** — no `spatialLedgers` namespace by default; ABSENCE is the dormant shape.
- **mint (at close, not per tick)** — one writer, one transaction, on the world the
  kernel RETURNS. ⚠ The EP volume's hard-won lesson binds verbatim: a stage that
  rebuilds the world wholesale between the last in-pulse read and the return leaves
  every in-pulse assertion green while the save, the undo snapshot, and the next
  composed tick all read an unstamped world. The pin asserts the ledger is present on
  the RETURNED world.
- **interval collapse (N→1)** — SURVIVES. `collapseIntervalHistory` composes by
  SPREAD, and `setSpatialLedger` preserves every sibling sub-key and its insertion
  order. ⚠ **A DECLARED NON-INVARIANT, stated at three homes so it is never
  re-diagnosed: ledger rows ≠ pulse-history length.** A 52-tick advance can credit
  many closes behind ONE surviving record. The pin asserts the counts are ALLOWED to
  differ, and a future "tidy-up" that teaches collapse to prune the ledger is a
  STOP-and-report.
- **commit to store** — survives; `cloneArray` is a shallow per-record spread, not an
  allowlist.
- **persist local / persist cloud** — whole-object JSON and a JSONB column with no
  projection. **FORCES: every value is a JSON scalar.** Never a Map, Set, Symbol,
  BigInt or Date in a stock, a pledge, or a key. The integer-stock choice (§3b)
  serves this path as well as determinism.
- **undo snapshot / restore** — habits vanish with the world on restore, and that is
  CORRECT: an un-lived outcome grades nothing. This path is what makes the epoch joint
  law true rather than merely compatible (§8.4).
- **paused cursor (mid-advance)** — ⚠⚠ THE ONE HAND-THREADED PATH. The ledger lives on
  `worldState` and needs no cursor field. **THE LAW: no habit credit may be computed
  mid-advance and consumed after the pause without being folded into `worldState`
  BEFORE the pause.** A resume that re-derives a habit-weighted draw from a world
  missing the pre-pause credit finishes as a DIFFERENT world than the half already
  committed — a silent mid-interval divergence, not a crash. HB-2's fence covers it.
- **worker transport** — automatic; plain scalars cross `structuredClone`. Zero
  transport edits, PROVIDED the JSON-scalar law holds.
- **account export** — survives (campaigns wholesale).
- **account IMPORT** — ⛔ **DROPPED. The entire `worldState` is replaced by a fresh
  default.** DECLARED, not silent:
  `src/lib/importReconciliationAdmission.js` carries the typed row
  `['worldState','world_state_not_imported','World simulation state']`. **HB-2 MUST
  SAY OUT LOUD that an imported campaign's actors have learned nothing** — which is
  correct, because a world with no lived history has no habits. ⚠ THE TRAP THIS
  CREATES AND MUST CLOSE: a doctrine sheet sold as a spy product across the import
  boundary reads an EMPTY ledger, and "no habits" must not render as "no tendencies".
  §3e's sheet returns ABSENCE, and the dossier renders absence as "this court has not
  yet shown its hand", never as a sheet of neutrals.
- **clone / duplicate** — n/a; MEASURED, no user-facing campaign duplicator exists.
- **migrate** — **n/a. No world migration and no `WORLD_STATE_SCHEMA_VERSION` bump is
  owed.** Absent-habit worlds ARE the legacy shape and read as habit-absent. The
  sub-ledger's own `v` field versions the sub-key independently, and a `v` the reader
  does not recognize heals the whole sub-ledger to ABSENT with a receipt (never
  partially) — the total-on-garbage discipline.
- **public payload / share / gallery** — ⛔ **NO, and NO NEW STRIP IS OWED.**
  MEASURED: `spatialLedgers` is already in `WORLD_SNAPSHOT_HARD_DENY`
  (`worldSnapshotPublic.js`), and `WORLD_SNAPSHOT_PUBLIC_LEDGER_ALLOWLIST` is
  `['pantheon']` alone; `tests/security/worldSnapshotDenyCensus.test.js` censuses the
  PARENT key, so a habit sub-key inherits the deny. ⚠⚠ **THIS IS LOAD-BEARING FOR THE
  COUNTERFORCE, not a formality: the truth ledger leaking to a public payload would
  hand every viewer perfect counter-intelligence for free and void the entire
  information war this design rests on.** The inherited deny is the right answer, and
  HB-2 lands an ANCHORED NEGATIVE that locks it rather than leaving it inherited and
  unstated.

**Single-writer census.** The habit sub-ledger has exactly ONE writer —
`habitLedger.js#writeHabits` — enforced by a shrink-only source-scan census with an
executed SECOND-WRITER PLANT, in the three-direction shape
`tests/lint/envoyErrandLedgerSingleWriter.walker.test.js` already uses: (1) the WRITER
EXACT SET both ways, so the declaration can never rot; (2) FUNCTION SCOPE — every write
form sits inside `writeHabits`'s own body, because a second function in the ledger leaf
would satisfy a file-level scan while breaking the law; (3) THE READERS READ — the
mention census must be strictly wider than the writer set, so the narrow WRITE regex is
provably not a broken pattern matching nothing.

**Coverage manifest.** `src/lib/spatialUsage.js` must classify `habits` as TRACKED or
EXEMPT **in the same commit as the first write**, or
`tests/lib/spatialLedgerCoverage.walker.test.js` (⚠ `tests/lib/`, NOT `tests/lint/` —
the EP volume shipped the wrong address at four sites before a chair ruling caught it)
REDS on day one: it asserts `expect(classified).toEqual(written)`, exact set equality
in both directions. **RULED TRACKED** (§6, J-HB-9), against census 4's tentative
EXEMPT: the routeNetwork and demographicPlans rows are TRACKED precisely because their
flags are virtual and lit in no preset, and `habitConditioningEnabled` will be exactly
that. Adoption is NOT legible from the flag when the flag is lit nowhere. ⛔ **The
store-layer escape is NAMED AND REFUSED**: `spatialUsage.js` states in-source that the
walker governs only keys written via `setSpatialLedger` inside `src/domain`, so a habit
stamp written from the store layer would evade it entirely. Routing around a
registration convention to save a manifest row is the failure the convention exists to
prevent.
