## §2 THE FLAG FAMILY — four flags, one strict ladder

**Name collision census, EXECUTED at HEAD.** All seven candidate tokens return ZERO
hits across `src/`: `habitStrength` · `habitConditioningEnabled` ·
`anticipationEnabled` · `doctrineTapEnabled` · `doctrineBands` · `circumstanceClass` ·
`HABIT_`. The namespace is free. ⚠ **BUT `momentum.js` WILL BE MISTAKEN FOR IT.**
`momentum` already ships a per-`(actorId, courseKey)` stock with its own decay, its own
cliff, and a bounded move multiplier consumed at the very seam habit wants
(`settlementStrategy`'s load block). The distinction must be stated in one sentence in
every habit module header and in the fork registry: **momentum is SUNK COST in a course
(path dependence); habit is LEARNED SUCCESS of an action in a circumstance class
(reinforcement). Different keys, different sources, different decay rationales, and
they compose multiplicatively without either one being the other's proxy.** ⛔ The FP
§10 protocol says "Never touch `momentum.js`" — this program does not, and the
distinction sentence is how a future lane is stopped from "unifying" them.

### 2.1 The four flags and why the cut falls here

| Flag | Wave | What it gates |
|---|---|---|
| `habitConditioningEnabled` | HB-2 | the ledger, the pledge book, the credit fold, and every chooser's habit term |
| `believedDoctrineEnabled` | HB-6 | the fourth SP-B subject family — `doctrineBands` on the belief record |
| `habitAnticipationEnabled` | HB-7 | the level-one anticipation term at the choosers, and the deviation damper |
| `doctrineTapEnabled` | HB-8 | the ES doctrine tap product, planted doctrine, and the doctrine-sheet Herald kind |

**Why four and not one.** Each flag gates a surface with a DIFFERENT owner and a
DIFFERENT risk profile: the ledger is engine state; the axis is a belief-record shape
change inside SP-B's family; anticipation is a behaviour change at foreign choosers;
the tap is a paid-surface-adjacent intelligence product. Lighting them together would
make the soak unable to attribute a distribution shift to a cause. **Why not more:**
the four map exactly to the four things that can be independently WRONG.

**Why `believedDoctrineEnabled` and not `doctrineAxisEnabled`.** The SP-B family is
spelled `believedScarcityEnabled` / `believedConditionsEnabled` /
`believedDevotionEnabled` (MEASURED in `ENGINE_GATED_VIRTUAL_RULE_KEYS`). A fourth
family that does not take the family spelling is a fourth family nobody finds. The
manifest is alphabetical, so `believedDoctrineEnabled` sorts directly beside its
siblings — which is the point.

### 2.2 The gate ladder (strict conjunctions, BY-NAME reads)

```js
// src/domain/worldPulse/habit/habitGate.js — the ONE door family

export function habitsActive(worldState) {
  const rules = worldState?.simulationRules || null;
  if (!rules) return false;
  return rules.habitConditioningEnabled === true;      // BY-NAME strict read
}

export function believedDoctrineActive(worldState) {
  if (!habitsActive(worldState)) return false;          // nothing to believe about
  if (!beliefAxesActive(worldState)) return false;      // SP-B's host axis flag
  const rules = worldState?.simulationRules || null;
  return rules?.believedDoctrineEnabled === true;       // BY-NAME strict read
}

export function habitAnticipationActive(worldState) {
  if (!believedDoctrineActive(worldState)) return false; // belief-routed by construction
  const rules = worldState?.simulationRules || null;
  return rules?.habitAnticipationEnabled === true;       // BY-NAME strict read
}

export function doctrineTapActive(worldState) {
  if (!believedDoctrineActive(worldState)) return false;
  if (!espionageActive(worldState)) return false;        // ES's own three-door gate
  const rules = worldState?.simulationRules || null;
  return rules?.doctrineTapEnabled === true;             // BY-NAME strict read
}
```

**THE CONJUNCTION-GATE HOLE (L2) is closed by construction:** every one of the four
keys is read BY NAME with the strict `=== true` idiom, exactly once, in this one file.
A frozen-list `.every()` would be a computed member access attributing to NO key —
fully wired, genuinely gated, and invisible to
`tests/lint/engineGatedRuleKeys.walker.test.js`. This is the SP-B recipe verbatim, and
SP-B's own header states the reason.

**THE ANTICIPATION FLAG IS BELIEF-ROUTED BY GATE, NOT BY DISCIPLINE.** Because
`habitAnticipationActive` cannot return true unless `believedDoctrineActive` does, and
that requires the belief axes, there is no configuration in which an anticipation term
can read anything but a belief record. The directive's "reading their BELIEF about the
other's habits, never the truth" is a STRUCTURAL property of the gate ladder, not a
convention a future edit could quietly violate. **⛔ The one thing that WOULD violate
it is a habit module importing `habitLedger` from the anticipation path** — pinned by a
zero-import scan on the anticipation leaf, the `outboundImpression` geometry.

**⛔ THE ORDER LAW.** `habitAnticipationEnabled` lights only after
`believedDoctrineEnabled`, which lights only after both `habitConditioningEnabled` and
`beliefAxesEnabled`; `doctrineTapEnabled` additionally requires the full espionage
conjunction. Out-of-order lighting is an invalid config the HB certification walker
reds. ⚠ MEASURED and inherited: `espionageActive` **cannot return true at HEAD** —
door 2 reads `rules.errandSpineEnabled !== true`, and while the TOKEN appears at EIGHT
`src` sites (four in `espionageGate.js` — three prose, one gate read — one inside a
cert-row prose string, and three in `tradeConvergenceContract.js` as a declared
foreign-flag dependency), **it is declared as a RULE KEY nowhere**: absent from
`DEFAULT_SIMULATION_RULES` and from `ENGINE_GATED_VIRTUAL_RULE_KEYS`, so no config can
set it and the strict `!== true` read always short-circuits. ⚠ **Census 3's phrasing
("exists nowhere in `src` except that line") was ALREADY overstated and is corrected
here** — the conclusion it drew is unchanged and is what matters: SP-D has not landed.
**CONSEQUENCE, stated
so no wave is architected on sand: `doctrineTapEnabled` is UNREACHABLE until SP-D
lands, and HB-8's tap arm therefore ships dark-forever until then.** That is why the
doctrine sheet's PRIMARY road is the SP-B axis fold and the dossier (both reachable
today), and the ES tap is the LATER enrichment, never the carrier.

### 2.3 The CQ5 trio, four times

Each flag joins `ENGINE_GATED_VIRTUAL_RULE_KEYS` (`simulationRules.js`, codepoint-
sorted, with an authored comment naming the wave, the ruling, the first gate read BY
SYMBOL, and the conjunction) **+ its AUTHORED certification row + its first real
by-name gate read, in ONE COMMIT**, with
`tests/lint/engineGatedRuleKeys.walker.test.js` asserting the exact one-key delta.

⛔ **THE ROW IS AUTHORED, NEVER PENDING.** MEASURED at HEAD: `VIRTUAL_PENDING_RULE_KEYS`
· `WAVE_PENDING_RULE_KEYS` · `BASELINE_PENDING_RULE_KEYS` · `PLACE_PENDING_RULE_KEYS`
are ALL `Object.freeze([])`. Direction 3 of the engine-gated walker exists precisely
because "manifested here, pending elsewhere" shipped a red on 2026-08-04. Manifesting
is the act that makes a virtual key censusable, so manifesting is the act that comes
due. Home: `src/domain/certification/subsystemRowsVirtual.js` — the ENGINE-GATED
VIRTUAL cohort, whose own header states the entry condition ("the key must be in
`ENGINE_GATED_VIRTUAL_RULE_KEYS`").

**THE ROW SHAPE, inherited from SP-B (J-SP-3: rows per FLAG, never per family).** A
conditional field inside another subsystem's container is invisible to every receipt
shape the estate writes — `censusWorldStateKeys` deliberately stops one level into
`spatialLedgers` and counts entries. So `believedDoctrineEnabled`'s row carries
`aliveness.{eventTypes, moverFamilies, stateKeys}` DELIBERATELY EMPTY with the whole
story in `other`, and `soakEvidence: 'unobserved'`. `habitConditioningEnabled`'s row
is the OPPOSITE and carries a real `stateKeys: ['spatialLedgers.habits']`, because the
habit ledger IS a top-level sub-key and IS censusable — the `vengeanceLicenses`
precedent, including its aliveness argument (use maxEntries rather than finalEntries,
because a ledger that filled and drained still proves the doctrine ran).

### 2.4 The DARK-STATE BYTE-IDENTITY contract

**The claim, stated as an obligation rather than a hope:** with all four flags absent,
a campaign's serialized bytes and its entire rng stream are IDENTICAL to a
pre-habit build, on every seed, at every tick, on every one of the FIFTEEN lifecycle
paths — **fifteen, not fourteen: the contract must also hold on `preview / forecast`, the
path this volume itself added, and a dark-state claim that stops one path short of its own
table is a claim with a hole in it.** Five mechanisms carry it, and each is pinned:

1. **NO KEY.** `habitsActive` false ⇒ the writer returns the input `worldState`
   REFERENCE unchanged (the `conquestMarchOrder` idiom: "the INPUT ARRAY REFERENCE,
   not a copy, not an equal array"), so `setSpatialLedger` — which is what CREATES the
   namespace — is unreachable. The gate returns FIRST, before any allocation.
2. **NO FACTOR.** `habitFactorFor` returns the number `1` on a dark world and on an
   absent row alike, through the same door. Multiplying by 1 is a no-op the estate
   already relies on at six existing load sites in the war chooser.
3. **NO DRAW.** MEASURED: `stableSampleByWeight` takes exactly one `rng.random()`
   regardless of the weights (`contestMath.js` — one roll, then a walk). Keyed races
   compute `weight * hash01(key)` where the key is per-candidate and INDEPENDENT of
   the weight. Softmax sites consume one sample. **In no idiom does a habit term change
   the number of draws or the values drawn** — only which index a fixed draw selects.
   Dark, even the index is unchanged, because the weights are unchanged.
4. **NO SPREAD.** The belief fold's arm table skips the `doctrineBands` arm entirely
   when the gate is null, so the returned record is byte-identical — SP-B's
   `foldSubjectAxes` shape, which returns a bare `{}` rather than an object of
   undefineds.
5. **NO STRING.** No receipt, news beat, dossier row, or prose token is composed on a
   dark path, so no golden moves.

⭐⭐ **REFINEMENT 10 — THE COLD-START FENCE: ONE ASSERTION PROVING THREE LAWS.** The
directive rules **COLD-START = DARK-STATE = NEUTRAL**, and the three are not three
fences but one, because all three go through the same door. The fence, named
`habitNeutralIdentity` and landed at HB-2:

> Take three worlds — (i) flags absent, ledger absent; (ii) flags LIT, ledger absent
> (a fresh campaign's first tick); (iii) flags LIT, ledger present but every stock
> exactly `NEUTRAL_I` before the drop pass — and assert that **every joined chooser's
> `habitLoad` is the NUMBER 1 in all three**, that the serialized world bytes of (i)
> and (ii) are IDENTICAL after a full advance, and that (iii) serializes to (ii)
> because drop-when-neutral removed every row.

The three laws it proves at once: **DARK is byte-identical** (i vs ii), **COLD-START is
habit-free by construction** (ii returns 1 with no special case — a fresh world's early
years are unconditioned because there is nothing to read, not because a guard says so),
and **NEUTRAL IS ABSENT** (iii collapses into ii, so "a ledger of zeros" is not a
representable state). ⚠ The reason to state it as ONE fence rather than three: three
separate fences invite three separate fixtures, and the third one always ends up
asserting a neutral ledger that the drop pass can no longer produce — a pin that
greens on an unreachable state. One fence, three worlds, one door.

**THE FOUR-FENCE SET × FOUR, WITH THE GUARD-DOOR COROLLARY.** Every flag ships the
four-fence dormancy set (own-footprint golden · absent-vs-false differential ·
call-path spy · gate-polarity census) **plus the LIT-MUTANT CONTROL** — without which,
in SP-B's own words, "the fence proves only that the fixture is quiet".

⚠ **THE GUARD-DOOR COROLLARY, and it is sharper here than at SP-B.** A guard gated on
OPTIONAL evidence CANNOT FIRE. `believedDoctrineActive` returns false when
`habitConditioningEnabled` is dark — so a fence that lights only
`believedDoctrineEnabled` and checks for a byte change proves NOTHING: the inner gate
was closed the whole time. **Each of the four fences must light its own flag AND every
flag beneath it in the ladder, and the lit-mutant must be taken at the DEEPEST gate the
fence claims to cover.** The gate-polarity census additionally asserts that each of the
four `Active` functions returns false when ANY of its conjuncts is absent, one conjunct
dropped at a time — a conjunction whose arms cannot be dropped one at a time is a
conjunction nobody has proven.

**⚠ CQ5 SERIALIZATION.** The manifest, the cert-row partition, and the walker test are
SHARED by every flag wave in one worktree, and `src/lib/spatialUsage.js` is shared by
every ledger-minting wave. Two concurrent flag waves DEADLOCK, and partial staging makes
lint-staged STASH. **HB's four flag waves are serialized against each other, against the
EPOCH volume's flag-and-ledger wave, and against any other in-flight flag wave — or one
worktree each.** Where a concurrent lane is unavoidable, build each shared file's staged
blob as `git show HEAD:path` plus your own blocks, stage via `update-index --cacheinfo`,
commit with NO pathspec, and PROVE the gate inside a `git archive` of the INDEX
(diff-hunk filtering FAILS: lanes insert at the same anchors).

---

## §3 THE MECHANISMS

Every constant below lands in ONE frozen exported `HABIT_TUNING` (the house idiom; L5
banded, owner-signed at the soak redo per THE PROMISE — **every value is a raw-authored
proposal until then, and NONE enters `proposedSoakBands.js`**). Every band's
reachability is MEASURED at authoring (the dead-band law — three shapes have bitten).
Zero new PRNG streams: the habit path is **entirely deterministic and takes no draw at
all**, which is a stronger statement than L1 requires and is the reason the replay
argument closes.

### 3a THE GRADING FOLD — credit at close, and the pledge book that makes it possible

**The problem, measured.** Every graded close in this estate is attributed to the
ACTOR, never to the ACTION. `dispositionLedger` is keyed (actorId → channel);
`DISPOSITION_SOURCE_KINDS` records WHAT KIND of outcome, not WHICH ACTION opened it.
The world knows "this court won a war"; it has no record of "this court chose DEPLOY
in circumstance X and it went well". Three of the six major lifecycle records
(occupations, trade wars, coup verdicts) carry no reference to the decision that opened
them at all — MEASURED: `createOccupationRecord` returns exactly
`{ occupierId, state, sinceTick, stateHeld, resistance, benefitYield, lastTick }`, and
a grep for `conquestId` inside `occupation.js` returns ZERO.

**THE PLEDGE BOOK is the answer, and it changes NO foreign record (R4).**

```
open: { <pledgeId>: [actorId, circumstanceClass, actionToken, openedWeek] }
                                                             // ⭐ openedWeek =
                                                             // worldState.calendar.elapsedWeeks
                                                             // (R10 / J-HB-17). NEVER a tick.

pledgeId = `${circumstanceClass}.${actorId}.${episodeKey}`
```

At a joined chooser, immediately after the action is selected and BEFORE any effect is
applied, the habit writer stamps a pledge. At the close, the closing site computes the
SAME `episodeKey` from its own record, looks up the pledge, grades it, applies the
credit, and DROPS the pledge. Four laws:

- **FIRST-WINS per pledgeId.** Re-deciding inside an open episode does not re-stamp
  (the epoch `byYear` precedent — never repaint a lived episode). The opening action is
  the one graded, which is exactly what refinement 4 asks for.
- **ONE PLEDGE, ONE GRADE, THEN GONE.** A pledge is consumed by its grade. A double
  grade is impossible because the second lookup misses.
- **LAPSE IS A RESULT.** A pledge older than `PLEDGE_MAX_AGE_WEEKS` (proposed 208 — four
  years on the campaign clock, ⭐ **WEEKS, NEVER TICKS — R10 / J-HB-17**, and the
  polish's P6 sweep corrected this sentence at its source) is dropped UNGRADED with a
  receipt. It teaches nothing. A war that never ends teaches nothing about how to start
  one, and that is honest.
- **THE PLEDGE BOOK IS BOUNDED.** ≤ one pledge per (actor, class, episode), plus the
  age sweep, plus a hard `PLEDGE_BOOK_CAP` with oldest-first deterministic eviction.

⭐⭐ **THE EPISODE KEY IS MINTED AT OPEN AND CARRIED — the chair's H3 ruling, and it
replaces the compile's recompute-at-both-ends claim.** The compile asserted that "the
close must be able to recompute the same `episodeKey` the open computed" and that this
"is satisfied at all seven joined closes". **THAT CLAIM FAILS AT CLOSE 1, THE FLAGSHIP
CLOSE, AND FAILING THERE IS FATAL** — the war chooser is the site the whole program is
built around. MEASURED at HEAD: the chooser runs at `rollCandidates`, one tick BEFORE
`warDeployment.evaluateWarLayer` step 4 mints the deployment; at the open
`deployment.sinceTick` does not exist and may never exist (the hard gates —
one-army, intervention-column, besieged-at-home, occupation, mobilization posture,
`HOSTILE_CONFIDENCE`, and the keystone `classifyFeasibility` — can all refuse a named
target, and "a row that was not obeyed expires silently"); at the close the disposition
delta is typed `Array<{id:string, outcome:'win'|'loss', magnitude?:number,
sourceConquestId?:string}>` with the in-source note "downstream applyDispositionDeltas
reads only {id, outcome, magnitude}" — **no pair key, no war id, no `sinceTick`.**
Neither side can compute the key, so every war pledge would MISS: not lapse with a
receipt, but permanently fail to join.

**THE RULING (J-HB-13). The episode key is a VALUE, minted once at the open and CARRIED
on the opening record, not a formula recomputed twice.** A key computed independently
at two sites is a coincidence maintained by hand; a key carried is a fact. Every close
whose opening artefact is a record HB can reach carries the key as ONE stamped field;
the remainder recompute, and the recompute set is now small enough to defend one row at
a time.

**THE CARRY ROAD FOR CLOSE 1, measured end to end.** The estate already ships the
carrier: the **WAR INTENT ORDER**. MEASURED, `warIntent.js` — `WAR_INTENT_LEDGER_KEY =
'warIntents'`, `@typedef {{ targetId: string, tick: number }} WarIntentRecord`,
`stampWarIntent(worldState, besiegerId, targetId, tick)`, `warIntentFor(worldState,
besiegerId, tick)`, `WAR_INTENT_TTL_TICKS = 2`; the chooser's order is applied by
`applyWorldPulse.js` (`applyWarIntentOutcome(state, outcome, tick ?? 0)`) at the SAME
tick it is emitted. MEASURED, `warDeployment.js` step 4 — `const marchOrder =
coalitionDecision ? null : warIntentFor(worldState, fromId, tick);` — **the opener holds
the order in hand at the exact moment it mints the deployment.** So:

1. **AT THE CHOOSER (open):** the pledge is stamped with
   `episodeKey = war:<besiegerId>:<targetId>:<orderTick>` — three values the chooser
   itself produced.
2. **AT THE OPENER (carry):** the deployment mint copies that key onto the new
   deployment record as ONE field, `habitEpisode`, recomputed from the order it is
   already reading. ⚠ `DeploymentRecord` is MEASURED as a deliberately loose bag
   (`@typedef {{ [k: string]: any }} DeploymentRecord`, with the in-source rationale),
   so this is an ADDITIVE field on an untyped record, drop-when-absent, written only
   under the habit flag.
3. **AT THE CLOSE:** `resolvedDeployments` carries `{ attackerId, deployment, targetId,
   outcome }` — the RECORD, not just the delta — so the close reads `habitEpisode` off
   the record it already has, with no change to `dispositionDeltas` and no new argument
   anywhere.
4. **AN ORDER THAT IS NEVER OBEYED** (refused by a hard gate, or expired at
   `WAR_INTENT_TTL_TICKS`) leaves a pledge nobody carries — which LAPSES on the age
   sweep with its receipt. **That is the correct semantics, not a leak:** a march the
   world refused teaches nothing about marching.

⛔ **THIS IS AN OWNER-GATED CLASS AND IT IS NAMED AS ONE, NOT SMUGGLED.** Adding a field
to a persisted campaign record is a persistence-shape move. Three facts make it the
cheapest available and they are stated so the owner can price the veto: the record is
already an untyped bag (no typedef change), the field is written only under
`habitConditioningEnabled` and dropped when absent (dark worlds byte-identical), and
the alternative — a parallel HB-side index from `(attackerId, targetId)` to a pledge —
is a SECOND index over a relation the deployment record already holds, which is the
ghost-write class wearing an architecture's clothing. **Recorded as chair question Q1
(re-aimed) and as J-HB-13's veto cost.**

**THE EIGHT JOINED CLOSES, AUDITED INTO CARRIED VS RECOMPUTED.** ⭐ **CLOSE 8 IS ADDED
AT THE POLISH (R18):** the goal close was live in the tree and joined at site 13 while
being absent from this table, so the volume's own "seven closes" denominator was never
reconciled against its joined-site table. Each row: the
circumstance, the episode key, HOW the key reaches the close, the closing vocabulary,
and the grade map. **Grades come ONLY from existing outcome vocabularies (refinement
12) — this program mints no judgment machinery.**

| # | Circumstance | Episode key | Key road | Closing vocabulary (existing) | Grade map |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | `war_open` / `siege_pressed` | `war:<besiegerId>:<targetId>:<orderTick>` | ⭐ **CARRIED** — stamped on the warIntent order at the chooser, copied to `deployment.habitEpisode` at `warDeployment` step 4, read off `resolvedDeployments[].deployment` at the close | the disposition delta `{outcome:'win'\|'loss', magnitude}` pushed by `warDeployment` | win ⇒ reinforce, loss ⇒ decay; `magnitude` banded into `SEVERITY_LADDER` by frozen edges |
| 2 | `terms_offered` | ⭐ **TWO KEY SHAPES, ONE CLOSE:** `terms:<treatyId>` for the site-8 accept/refuse episode, and `terms:<offererId>:<targetId>:<peaceFrontSinceTick>` for the site-7 keyed sue episode | ⭐⭐ **BOTH ROADS — CORRECTED AT THE ATTESTATION; "no carry owed" WAS TRUE OF ONLY HALF THE CLOSE.** **(i) RECOMPUTED** for `warPeaceDecision`'s accept/refuse episodes: the treaty id IS the episode and both sides hold it, so nothing is carried and nothing is stamped. **(ii) CARRIED** for `settlementStrategy`'s keyed `sue_for_peace` episodes: the chooser cannot know the `treatyId` at the open, so the key is minted from the three values the `bilateralOffer` spread emits and stamped onto the treaty record at mint as `treaty.habitEpisode` (J-HB-13's road, gated by J-HB-24). ⚠ **THE TWO ROADS TAKE TWO DIFFERENT PINS AND NEITHER COVERS THE OTHER** — the recompute-equality fixture for (i), the carry ROUND-TRIP fixture for (ii). A close that reaches its grades by two roads is not a recomputed close with a footnote | `treatyDisposition`'s `treaty_held` / `treaty_default` / `mediation_landed` (MEASURED: three `sourceKind` literals in that module) **plus `treaty_repudiated`, pushed from `treatyBreach.js`** — ⚠ TWO MODULES, one vocabulary; the grade map imports BOTH | held ⇒ telling reinforce · mediation_landed ⇒ glancing reinforce · default ⇒ grave decay · repudiated ⇒ ruinous decay |
| 3 | `coalition_called` | `coalition:<episodeKey>` | **RECOMPUTED** — `compromiseRound` already keys on this episode and the close reads the same key | the five coalition source kinds: reimbursement paid/unpaid, settlement profit/honored/shortfall | paid·profit·honored ⇒ reinforce (telling/grave/telling) · unpaid·shortfall ⇒ decay (grave/telling) |
| 4 | `crowding_pressed` | the plan's own `episode` (`'<tick>:<band>'`) | ⭐ **CARRIED ALREADY, BY THE ESTATE** — the plan record stamps it at open; HB reads it and stamps nothing. **This is the reference implementation, and it is the proof the carry road is the estate's own idiom rather than this volume's invention** | `PLAN_OUTCOMES` (6, MEASURED: `delivered, ground_lost, headroom_lost, lane_dark, pressure_eased, unaffordable`) | delivered ⇒ telling reinforce · pressure_eased ⇒ grave reinforce · headroom_lost ⇒ telling decay · ground_lost ⇒ grave decay · unaffordable ⇒ glancing decay · **lane_dark ⇒ NO GRADE (lapse)** — the world refused, not the choice |
| 5 | `trade_contested` | `trade:<prizeId>:<contestOpenTick>` | **RECOMPUTED — ⚠ VERIFY-AT-BUILD, AND THE STOP IS REAL.** The prize id is stable; `contestOpenTick` must be shown reachable at the close from the contest record. **If it is not, this close CARRIES on the same road as close 1 or it does not join** — the compile's "satisfied at all seven" is exactly the sentence that failed once already | the `trade_contest` disposition delta (flip vs hold) | flip-toward-us ⇒ telling reinforce · flip-away ⇒ telling decay · hold ⇒ glancing reinforce for the incumbent |
| 6 | `faith_contested` | `faith:<traditionRecordId>:<openTick>` | ⭐ **CARRIED** — the tradition record persists across the observance and the open stamps `habitEpisode` on it; the close already reads that record to write `lastOutcome` | **`TRADITION_OUTCOME` — SIX, not three (R12).** MEASURED under the in-source heading "THE SIX-OUTCOME VOCABULARY": `triumph, good, modest, troubled, failure, cancelled` | triumph ⇒ grave reinforce · good ⇒ telling reinforce · modest ⇒ glancing reinforce · troubled ⇒ glancing decay · failure ⇒ grave decay · **cancelled ⇒ NO GRADE (lapse)** — the observance did not happen, on the `lane_dark` precedent |
| 7 | *(ES-gated)* `war_open`/`rival_ascendant` espionage dispatch | `mission:<errandId>` | **RECOMPUTED** — the errand id is the episode on both sides | `MISSION_GRADES` (4) with `MISSION_GRADE_ORDER` | exceeded ⇒ grave reinforce · met ⇒ telling reinforce · partial ⇒ glancing decay · empty ⇒ grave decay |
| ⭐ 8 | `unpressed` + the live class — the NPC goal | `goal:<goalId>` | ⭐ **CARRIED ALREADY, BY THE ESTATE** — the `LadderGoal` record persists with its OWN mint baseline and HB stamps nothing. MEASURED: `npcLadderGoals.evaluateGoal(goal, frame)` returns `{ progress, value, readable, fired }` with `progress = progressOf(num(goal.startScore, value), value, threshold)` — *"Progress is computed from the goal's stored mint baseline (startScore→threshold), so it is fully deterministic and serializable"* | a CONTINUOUS `progress ∈ [0,1]` plus `fired`, and an unreadable signal returning `readable: false` — the leaf's own in-source *"the premise LAPSED (honest null — the kernel reminds without reward/penalty)"* | ⚠ **THE GRADE MAP IS A BANDING, NOT A LOOKUP, AND THAT IS DECLARED:** `fired === true` ⇒ reinforce, banded from `progress` by FROZEN EDGES into `SEVERITY_LADDER`; abandoned-below-threshold ⇒ decay, same edges; **`readable === false` ⇒ LAPSE** (the honest null, on the `lane_dark` precedent). ⛔ A float grade may never enter the curve un-banded — the edges are `HABIT_TUNING` members and their reachability is measured like every other band |

⭐⭐ **THE CARRY-ROAD PARTITION, RE-PARSED AT THE ATTESTATION FROM THE TABLE ABOVE RATHER
THAN INHERITED: CARRIED {1, 4, 6, 8} · RECOMPUTED {3, 7} · ⭐ BOTH ROADS {2} · DECLARED
VERIFY-AT-BUILD {5} — four buckets over eight closes, and they sum.** ⚠ **THE OLD
SPELLING PUT CLOSE 2 IN THE RECOMPUTED BUCKET FLAT**, which was true of the site-8
accept/refuse episode and false of the site-7 keyed sue episode that P3 joined and
J-HB-24 gated — **the same close reaches its grades by two roads, and a partition that
cannot say so hands an implementer one pin where two are owed.** Three of the four
carried closes (4, 6 and 8) carry on records the estate already persists and mutates at
the close, so the stamped field costs nothing new; **only close 1 and close 2's SUE ROAD
add a field to a foreign record** — `deployment.habitEpisode` and `treaty.habitEpisode`
— and those two rows are what J-HB-13 and Q1 put in front of the owner.

⭐⭐ **THE ACTION-COVERAGE LAW (new at the polish, and it is the structural cure the
recohere's HIGH 2 / HIGH 3 pair asked for).** The grade-map totality law below asserts
that a map covers its CLOSING VOCABULARY. It says nothing about whether the ACTIONS a
site can take are covered by any close at all — and that is the gap R18 found:

> **EVERY ACTION IN EVERY JOINED SITE'S VOCABULARY IS EITHER (a) NAMED IN A CLOSE ROW,
> or (b) CARRIES AN EXPLICIT `NEEDS-A-CLOSE` DEFERRAL ROW WITH A WRITTEN `closeOwed`.
> There is no third state. A site whose actions are ALL in state (b) is a DEFER site,
> not a LEARN site, whatever its seam sentence says. The walker asserts the union of
> every joined site's action vocabulary equals `covered ∪ needsClose`, exact set
> equality both directions, and that `needsClose` is SHRINK-ONLY.**
>
> ⭐ **AMENDED (J-HB-24) — AN ACTION MAY BE IN BOTH STATES, ON DIFFERENT PATHS, AND THAT
> IS STILL NOT A THIRD STATE.** Where a close is reachable only behind a measured gate,
> the action takes a COVERED row naming the gate AND a `NEEDS-A-CLOSE` row for the
> ungated path, with its own `closeOwed`. The law is a UNION, never a partition, so
> `covered ∪ needsClose` still equals the vocabulary exactly. ⛔ **What is forbidden is
> the flat spelling — an action written COVERED when its episode mints only sometimes,
> which is how an implementer builds an unconditional stamp against a conditional
> emitter.** `sue_for_peace` is the first and so far only such action.

Two reasons it is stated as a law rather than as a table caption. **First, it is the
instrument that would have caught MEDIUM 5 as well** — a site dispositioned LEARN
against a symbol that is not a chooser has, by construction, no action vocabulary to
cover, and the law's set equality reds on an empty left-hand side. **Second, and this is
the honest half: a `LEARN` row whose actions have no close is a DEFER row wearing a
LEARN row's clothing**, and the volume shipped six of them.

⭐⭐ **THE CLOSE-COVERAGE TABLE, PER ACTION, AT EVERY JOINED SITE.** ⚠ Read the third
column as the disposition; a site is LEARN only where at least one action is COVERED.

| Site | Action | Close | Coverage |
|---|---|---|---|
| 7 `settlementStrategy` | `deploy` | close 1 (CARRIED, `war:<besiegerId>:<targetId>:<orderTick>`) | ⭐ **COVERED** — and MEASURED as the ONLY covered move: `applyWarIntentOutcome` stamps the order only under `metadata.strategyMove === 'deploy' && metadata.deployTargetId != null`, and `settlementStrategy`'s `if (move === 'deploy')` branch is the sole emitter of that metadata |
| 7 | `sue_for_peace` — ⭐ **THE BILATERALLY-KEYED PATH ONLY** | ⭐ close 2 (the TREATY grades) | ⭐ **COVERED — CONDITIONALLY, AND THE CONDITION IS THE JOIN (J-HB-24). LEARN-WHEN-KEYED, never "covered" flat.** The chair's P3 ruling stands — where an existing grade already covers an action, JOIN it — but the join is only available where the episode key can be minted, and MEASURED at HEAD `3e2bd309` (blob `43ab53f9`, `src/domain/worldPulse/settlementStrategy.js`, by SYMBOL `emitMove`) **the three key values are emitted behind a gate, not unconditionally.** ⭐ **THE LIVE GATE, QUOTED VERBATIM:** `const warRulingsLit = worldState?.simulationRules?.warLayerEnabled === true && worldState?.simulationRules?.warTerminationEnabled === true;` and, inside the sue branch, `const peaceFront = worldState?.deployments?.[String(sId)]; const peaceFrontSinceTick = inputTick(peaceFront?.sinceTick); const bilateralOffer = warRulingsLit && !!termination && String(peaceFront?.targetId \|\| '') === String(target) && peaceFrontSinceTick != null;` — and the payload spread is `...(bilateralOffer ? { peaceOffer: true, offererId: String(sId), targetId: String(target), peaceFrontOwnerId: String(sId), peaceFrontSinceTick, } : {}),`. **THE FIELDS EXIST EXACTLY WHEN `bilateralOffer` IS TRUE AND AT NO OTHER TIME.** ⭐ **THE RULE, THEREFORE: the episode is minted IFF `bilateralOffer`.** The arm's counterpart resolution is real (`const target = terminationTarget \|\| besiegingTarget \|\| ctx.hostileTargets[0] \|\| ctx.besieging[0];`) and the treaty grades are real as four source kinds (`treaty_held` / `treaty_default` / `mediation_landed` from `treatyDisposition.js`, `treaty_repudiated` from `treatyBreach.js` — MEASURED, all four also in `dispositionLedger.js`'s source-kind list). ⚠⚠ **AND IT CARRIES ON THE SAME ROAD AS CLOSE 1, WHICH IS A SECOND PERSISTED FIELD:** the chooser cannot know the `treatyId`, so the key is minted at the open as `terms:<offererId>:<targetId>:<peaceFrontSinceTick>` — the three values the gated spread emits — and stamped onto the treaty record at mint as `habitEpisode`. **THIS WIDENS Q1 FROM ONE FOREIGN FIELD TO TWO** and is put in front of the owner as such, never smuggled as an implementation detail of a join. ⛔ **Q1's two-field widening STANDS AS DECLARED** — the gate narrows WHEN the second field is written, never WHETHER the schema carries it |
| 7 | `sue_for_peace` — ⛔ **THE UNKEYED PATH (`bilateralOffer === false`)** | — | ⛔ **NEEDS-A-CLOSE — AN EXPLICIT DEFERRAL ROW, WRITTEN RATHER THAN ABSORBED (J-HB-24).** A peace sued with the war rulings dark, or with no `termination` read, or from a court whose own deployment front does not point at the resolved `target`, or with an unresolvable `sinceTick`, emits NONE of the five fields — so **no episode key can be minted, NO episode is minted, and the move receives NO credit and NO penalty.** It is a LAPSE by construction, not a silent one: the chooser takes the habit factor it always takes and the grading fold never opens a pledge. ⚠ **THIS IS THE HONEST HALF OF THE JOIN AND IT IS RECORDED HERE SO THAT NO IMPLEMENTER READS "sue_for_peace IS COVERED" AND BUILDS AN UNCONDITIONAL STAMP.** `closeOwed`: *"a peace sued without a bilateral front is CLOSELESS today; the close is owed when a GENERAL OFFER RECORD exists — a persisted record of the suit itself, independent of `worldState.deployments`, against which the suit's acceptance, refusal or lapse can be graded. HB-9 owes it; this program does not mint one, because minting a general offer record is a persisted-shape change nobody has signed."* ⭐ The pin is an ANCHORED NEGATIVE: a sue emitted with `bilateralOffer` false produces ZERO habit pledges |
| 7 | `defend` · `hold` · `reroute` · `embargo` · `credit` · `missionize` · `legitimacy` · `prestige` · `opportunity` (NINE) | — | ⛔ **NEEDS-A-CLOSE.** Nine deferral rows, each with its own `closeOwed`. The two sharpest, written out because HB-9 owes them by name: `defend` — *"the siege's own outcome (`STATE_LADDER` relief vs fall) graded against the tick the court chose to defend rather than sue"*; `hold` — *"the absence of a front over a stated horizon, graded against the rival's realized posture — an ABSTENTION close, which the estate has never minted and which is the honest reason `hold` cannot join today"* |
| 1 `demographicsResponses` | all six `RESPONSES` | close 4 (`PLAN_OUTCOMES`, CARRIED by the estate) | ⭐ **COVERED — ⚠ VERIFY-AT-BUILD that every one of the six opens a plan.** The plan record is the reference implementation and stamps the `response` chosen, but "every response opens a plan" is an assumption the polish did NOT execute. If a response exists that opens no plan, it takes a `NEEDS-A-CLOSE` row rather than riding the site's coverage |
| 3 `coalitionRatification` | both `BALLOT_DECISIONS` | close 3 (RECOMPUTED, the coalition episode) | **COVERED** |
| 6 `tradeWar` | the typed posture set (contest / hold) | close 5 (⚠ the declared VERIFY-AT-BUILD) | **COVERED IF CLOSE 5 CARRIES.** If `contestOpenTick` is not reachable at the close, close 5 CARRIES on close 1's road or site 6 becomes DEFER — the fallback was already named and now it has a coverage consequence |
| 8 `warPeaceDecision` | `{accept, refuse}` | close 2 (RECOMPUTED, `terms:<treatyId>` — both sides hold the id here) | **COVERED** |
| 13 `npcLadderGoals` | the faction-domain goal kinds | ⭐ close 8 (CARRIED by the estate) | **COVERED** |
| 2 `generosityKernel`'s caller | all four `VERDICTS` | — | ⛔ **NEEDS-A-CLOSE — THE SITE DEMOTES TO DEFER (R18).** MEASURED, the raw material exists and is exactly why the close is delicate: the kernel pushes `receipts.push({ giverId, receiverId, verdict: verdict.verdict, magnitude })` and mints `reliefIncident({ kind: 'relief_given' \| 'relief_refused', … })`, and `generosityEV.historyTerm(history)` ALREADY consumes those incidents (`if (history.betrayal === true) return { value01: 0, betrayalKill: true };`). `closeOwed`: *"grade the give verdict against the recipient's realized reciprocity or betrayal over a stated horizon, WITHOUT re-reading the incidents `historyTerm` already consumes — the double-count fence §3c states is a CLOSE-DESIGN constraint, not only a load-point one"* |
| 10 `advanceIntervention`'s caller | `{engage, decline}` | — | ⛔ **NEEDS-A-CLOSE — THE SITE DEMOTES TO DEFER (R18).** `closeOwed`: *"the intervention's own resolution (relieved / repelled / withdrawn) graded against the engage decision that opened it"* |
| 12 `sovereigntyMarketStage` | the typed offer posture | — | ⛔ **NEEDS-A-CLOSE — THE SITE DEMOTES TO DEFER (R18).** `closeOwed`: *"the sale's completion or collapse graded against the offer posture, on the WR-10 ledger-membership road"* |

⭐ **THE JOINED SET IS THEREFORE SIX SITES, NOT TWELVE, AND THE HONEST GAP GREW BY
THREE.** That is not a retreat — **it is the graded-close precondition finally applied to
the volume's own table instead of only to the census's.** The scarce resource was always
graded outcomes attributable to an opening action; the polish is the first pass that
counted them per ACTION rather than per SITE.

⭐⭐ **THE GRADE-MAP TOTALITY LAW (new, and R12 is why it exists).** *Every grade map is
TOTAL over its closing vocabulary. The map imports the vocabulary and a walker asserts
EXACT KEY EQUALITY in both directions; a rung with no entry is a RED, never a silent
lapse.* Close 6 shipped a three-of-six map in the compile precisely because the map was
authored from a remembered vocabulary rather than an imported one. **`LAPSE` is a
first-class map value** — `lane_dark` and `cancelled` are both explicitly mapped to it —
so "this outcome teaches nothing" is a stated decision with a receipt, and the absence
of an entry can never be mistaken for one.

⚠ **CLOSE 4 IS THE REFERENCE AND IT IS ALREADY BUILT.** `demographicsPlans` persists a
plan carrying, at OPEN, the `response` chosen, the `episode` (`'<tick>:<band>'`), the
`band` at the crossing, the `openedTick`, the winning `weight`, and the `because[]`
reasons — and closes with a six-word outcome vocabulary whose abandon rule explicitly
compares the current band against "the band that opened it". **HB-3 copies this record
shape and this seam; it does not invent one.** Where the pledge book and the plan record
both carry the open, the PLAN is the authority and the pledge is a pointer — one truth,
two indexes.

⚠ **CLOSE 2 CARRIES THE ESTATE'S SHARPEST CORRECTNESS FORK.** `evolveCompliance`
returns BOTH `trueState` and `observedState`, and `observedState` ghosts to `honored`
whenever `monitorReach01 < DETECT_FLOOR` — "a poorly-informed victor sees honored while
the loser cheats". **EVERY HABIT FED FROM TREATY COMPLIANCE READS `observedState`.**
Reading `trueState` would let an actor learn from a cheat it never detected — an
omniscience leak that also defeats the belief-routing law this whole program rests on.
HB-3 pins it with a mutant that flips the read to `trueState` and reds.

⭐⭐ **THE GRADED-CLOSE PRECONDITION, STATED AS LAW (refinement 15) — and this is the
honest half of the program.** The directive's own words become the volume's governing
sentence for every fork that does not join:

> **A fork joins the learning system EXACTLY WHEN its circumstance has a GRADED CLOSE.
> Credit-at-close is the law; where there is no grade there is nothing honest to learn
> from. A fork without a close is not excluded — it is ENUMERATED, deferred-with-reason,
> visible on the books, with close-minting named as future work. TOTAL IN PRINCIPLE,
> GATED BY GRADEABILITY IN PRACTICE, AND THE GAP IS NEVER SILENT.**

Census 1 dispositioned fifty decision sites: 14 HABIT-READY, 30 NEEDS-A-CLOSE, 6
NOT-A-DECISION. **The scarce resource is graded outcomes attributable to an opening
action, not places to apply a weight** — and the thirty are therefore not a shortfall,
they are **THE DEFERRAL BOOK**, the program's published account of what it cannot yet
honestly learn from. ⭐ **THE BOOK IS FORTY-THREE ROWS AT THIS REVISION, AND EVERY
ADDITION IS A CORRECTION RATHER THAN A REGRESSION. THE DECOMPOSITION IS 30 + 3 + 10:**
the census's 30, plus THREE site rows demoted from LEARN by the action-coverage law
(sites 2, 10, 12 — R18), plus TEN per-action rows — the NINE strategy moves that close 1
cannot reach (R17/R18) **and the UNKEYED `sue_for_peace` path, which J-HB-24 gave its own
explicit `NEEDS-A-CLOSE` row and which the FORTY-TWO spelling never counted.** ⚠⚠ **THE
FORTY-TWO WAS STALE FOR EXACTLY THE REASON J-HB-23 NAMES: J-HB-24 ADDED A ROW TO §3a's
COVERAGE TABLE AND THE BOOK'S DENOMINATOR WAS NOT RE-PARSED IN THE SAME EDIT.** The
figure is now an EXECUTED SELF-ASSERTION, derived from the coverage table by
`countsweep.py` (the COUNT CENSUS appendix) rather than read off a previous
revision. ⚠ **THREE FURTHER SITES LEFT
THE BOOK ENTIRELY IN THE OTHER DIRECTION AND ARE NOT DEFER ROWS AT ALL** — sites 4, 5 and
14 are STAY-DETERMINISTIC (R13, R14, R15), because a fork that does not exist cannot be
deferred; deferring it would put a close on the books that nothing could ever consume.

**THE DEFERRAL BOOK IS A PUBLISHED ARTEFACT, NOT A TEST FIXTURE.** ⚠ **ALL FORTY-THREE
ROWS — the whole book, not the census's thirty — live** in `habitForkRegistry.js` with
`{forkId, module, symbol, disposition:
'DEFER', reason, closeOwed, closeSource}` where **`closeOwed` is a SENTENCE naming the
close that would let the row join** ("arrival-vs-`banditryLoss` on the chosen path",
"corridor survival vs `routeNetworkDecay` retirement", "the occupation `STATE_LADDER`
graded against the pledge key") — never the empty string, never "TBD". A DEFER row
whose `closeOwed` is unwritable is not a deferral, it is a STAY, and it takes a STAY
disposition with its reason. ⚠ THE WALKER ENFORCES BOTH HALVES: `DEFER` is
SHRINK-ONLY (a row may leave the defer list, never join it), **and every `DEFER` row
must carry a non-empty `closeOwed`** — which is what stops the defer list from becoming
the place inconvenient forks go to be forgotten. ⚠ **CORRECTED AT THE POLISH (P8): the
registry leaf IS the publication.** The amendment said the rows were "generated from this
registry" into §7 and §7 is five chair questions — a deferral book with no home is a
deferral book nobody reads. `habitForkRegistry.js` is a repo file a reader can grep and a
walker can enumerate, which is strictly better than a document section, and the volume
points at it rather than mirroring it.

That enumeration is **the fork registry** (§3c), and the closes owed are HB-9's charter:

- **occupation outcomes** — `STATE_LADDER` (5 rungs) is a real graded ladder; what is
  missing is only the episode key, and the pledge book supplies it
  — ⚠ **RE-AIMED BY J-HB-13: the compile proposed
  `occupation:<settlementId>:<sinceTick>` with "both sides can compute it", which is the
  SAME SENTENCE THAT FAILED AT CLOSE 1.** MEASURED, `createOccupationRecord` returns
  exactly `{ occupierId, state, sinceTick, stateHeld, resistance, benefitYield, lastTick
  }` and a grep for `conquestId` in `occupation.js` returns ZERO — the record has a
  `sinceTick` but the OPENING DECISION does not know it, because the occupation is
  minted by the conquest, not by the chooser. **THE OCCUPATION CLOSE THEREFORE CARRIES
  ON THE SAME ROAD AS CLOSE 1** (the key rides from the chooser through the conquest onto
  `occupationRecord.habitEpisode`) or it does not join. **CLOSE MINTABLE, HB-9, ON THE
  CARRY ROAD — and HB-9 re-measures the carry chain before building, because a
  recompute claim here would be the third instance of the same defect.**
- **route outcomes** — `banditryLoss` realizes a loss on a chosen path but nothing
  grades it back to the choice. A close is mintable from arrival-vs-loss on the chosen
  route. **CLOSE MINTABLE, HB-9, and it is the ROUTE CHOICE row refinement 17 demands
  by name.**
- **instrument choice** (`supplyWebWarfare.chooseInstrument`), **captor/home ransom
  answers**, **the NPC verdict table**, **conquest intent**, **envoy private goals**,
  **the interception decision** — each carries a closed action vocabulary and NO close.
  DEFER rows with the close named and its cost stated.
- **guard chains with no weighted arm** — `razingGate`, `captorRansomChoice`,
  `homeRansomAnswer`, `deliberationRead`. ⛔ **RULED STAY-DETERMINISTIC (J-HB-6).**
  Adding a weighted arm to a pure guard chain is a BEHAVIOUR CHANGE, not a habit input:
  it would move dark-world bytes and break the four-fence contract. Habit cannot tilt a
  guard, and pretending otherwise would convert legality into preference.
  ⚠ `razingGate` is the sharpest case and deserves its sentence: an alignment gate is
  LEGALITY, not preference; the most habit could ever move there is the DETERRENCE leg,
  and even that is deferred rather than taken.

### 3b THE FROZEN CURVE — reinforcement, decay, and the two fences

**All constants raw-authored proposals. NONE ratified. NONE in `proposedSoakBands.js`.**

```
NEUTRAL_I = 5000            // the neutral stock, in 1e-4 integer units
FLOOR_I   = 1500            // 0.15 — the tilt floor
CAP_I     = 8500            // 0.85 — the tilt cap
HABIT_SPAN        = 0.35    // the chooser multiplicand's half-span
ANTICIPATION_SPAN = 0.20    // the observer's term is WEAKER than self-knowledge
DEVIATE_W         = 0.60    // how much awareness of being watched damps own habit
SEAT_SHOCK_KEEP   = 0.50    // a new seat keeps half the distance from neutral

LEARN_RATE   = { lawless: 0.22, balanced: 0.14, lawful: 0.09 }   // refinement 16
HALF_LIFE    = { lawless: 'a_year', balanced: 'a_few_years', lawful: 'a_decade' }
SEVERITY_W   = { glancing: 0.35, telling: 1.00, grave: 1.40, ruinous: 0.80 }
```

**THE UPDATE, in one expression:**

```
ageWeeks = nowWeeks - w          // BOTH from worldState.calendar.elapsedWeeks (R10)
decayed  = decayTowardNeutral(s, NEUTRAL, ageWeeks, HALF_LIFE[lawWord])   // bandedStock
shocked  = seatAdjusted(decayed, entrySeat, currentSeat)                  // refinement 8
step     = LEARN_RATE[lawWord] * SEVERITY_W[severity]
next     = dir > 0 ? shocked + step * (CAP - shocked)
                   : shocked - step * (shocked - FLOOR)
s'       = roundToUnits(next)                                             // THE FENCE
```

⭐⭐ **THE STOCK → FACTOR MAPPING — stated here because the compile never stated it
anywhere, and every one of the H1/H2 findings traced back to its absence.** A stock is
an integer in `[FLOOR_I, CAP_I]` centred on `NEUTRAL_I`; a factor is a dimensionless
multiplier centred on 1. The map is ONE affine expression, in ONE function
(`habitFactor`), with no second spelling:

```
tilt01   = (sRounded - NEUTRAL_I) / (CAP_I - NEUTRAL_I)   // ∈ (-1, +1), asymptotic
factor   = 1 + effectiveSpan * tilt01                      // effectiveSpan per §3f
```

At `FLOOR_I` (1500) and `CAP_I` (8500) the arithmetic is deliberately ASYMMETRIC in
STOCK space and SYMMETRIC in FACTOR space: `(1500 - 5000) / 3500 = -1.0` and
`(8500 - 5000) / 3500 = +1.0`, so the factor's reachable open interval is exactly
`(1 - HABIT_SPAN, 1 + HABIT_SPAN)` in both directions. ⚠ **This is why FLOOR and CAP are
equidistant from NEUTRAL and must stay so**; a tuning pass that moves one without the
other silently makes reinforcement and decay different-sized instruments, which is a
behaviour change disguised as a dial. The walker asserts `NEUTRAL_I - FLOOR_I === CAP_I
- NEUTRAL_I` and names the reason in its failure message.

**TILT-NEVER-LOCK IS STRUCTURAL, NOT CLAMPED.** The update moves a FRACTION of the
remaining distance to the bound, so the bound is asymptotic and **unattainable in
finite steps**. A clamp is retained as a belt-and-braces against arithmetic surprise,
but it is never the thing doing the work. This matters: a clamped-linear update reaches
the cap and then LOCKS, which is the dead-arm law's failure mode wearing a cap's
clothing.

⭐⭐⭐ **THE ODDS-RATIO LAW — THE P1 CLOSURE, AND IT IS THE SHARPEST CORRECTION EITHER
ROUND HAS MADE, BECAUSE IT IS THE ONLY ONE THAT IS MATHEMATICAL RATHER THAN
MEASURED.** The amendment's pin — `p'_i / p_i` strictly inside
`(1 − HABIT_SPAN, 1 + HABIT_SPAN)`, "which the renormalized form gives by construction,
since the renormalizer is a convex mean of the factors" — **is UNSATISFIABLE, and the
by-construction argument is FALSE.** The convex-mean fact is true and irrelevant: under
`p'_i = p_i·f_i / Σ p_j·f_j` the per-probability ratio is

```
p'_i / p_i  =  f_i / mean_f          where mean_f = Σ p_j·f_j  ∈  [min f, max f]
```

and because `mean_f` can sit anywhere in `[min f, max f]`, that ratio ranges over
`[(1−s)/(1+s), (1+s)/(1−s)]` — **`[0.4815, 2.0769]` at `s = HABIT_SPAN = 0.35`, not
`(0.65, 1.35)`.** The bound is ATTAINED, not merely approached, whenever one candidate
sits at the cap and the rest at the floor — which is precisely the state the design
produces (a conditioned court). **⛔ THE PER-PROBABILITY CLAIM IS DELETED AS FALSE. It is
not narrowed, not qualified, and not kept as an aspiration: a pin that reds on the
design's own intended state is worse than no pin, because two waves (HB-0 and HB-5) were
chartered to land it as the law's proof.**

> ⭐⭐ **TILT-NEVER-LOCK, RESPELLED (J-HB-18). THE INVARIANT IS ON PAIRWISE ODDS
> RATIOS.** For any two candidates `i` and `j` at any joined site:
>
> ```
> (p'_i / p'_j) / (p_i / p_j)   ∈   [ (1 − s) / (1 + s) ,  (1 + s) / (1 − s) ]
> ```
>
> where `s = HABIT_SPAN`. **No option's odds against any other option may move by more
> than that factor, in either direction, ever — and no option's probability may be
> driven to 0 or to 1.**

**THE BY-CONSTRUCTION ARGUMENT, RESTATED ON FACTOR RATIOS, WHICH IS WHERE IT IS ACTUALLY
TRUE.** Under `p'_i = p_i·f_i / Z` with the SAME `Z = Σ p_j·f_j` for every candidate:

```
p'_i / p'_j  =  (p_i·f_i / Z) / (p_j·f_j / Z)  =  (p_i / p_j) · (f_i / f_j)
```

**`Z` CANCELS.** The odds ratio is therefore EXACTLY `f_i / f_j`, and since every factor
is a bounded per-candidate multiplicand in the open interval `(1 − s, 1 + s)`, the ratio
of any two lies in the open interval `((1−s)/(1+s), (1+s)/(1−s))`. ⭐ **This is the
argument the amendment reached for and could not have on the quantity it chose:
normalization destroys the per-probability bound and preserves the pairwise one, because
a common divisor is invisible to a ratio of ratios and fatal to a ratio.** The
tilt-never-lock property the law is about — *no option is ever driven to zero* — is a
statement about RELATIVE standing between candidates, so the relative measure is the one
that carries it.

⭐⭐ **THE ANCHOR-SITE HAND-WALK, EXECUTED AT THE REAL `STRATEGY_K = 3.5` (MEASURED:
`settlementStrategy.js` `const STRATEGY_K = 3.5;` and `const weights =
softmaxWeights(decisionMoves.map((m) => m.score), STRATEGY_K);`).** Four martial moves,
scores `[0.90, 0.85, 0.80, 0.20]` for `[defend, deploy, hold, sue_for_peace]`, one action
at CAP and three decayed to FLOOR — **exactly the conditioned court the design
produces:**

```
p_dark        = [0.3802, 0.3191, 0.2679, 0.0328]
factors       = [0.6500, 1.3500, 0.6500, 0.6500]
p_habit       = [0.2829, 0.4933, 0.1994, 0.0244]

per-probability ratios   = [0.7442, 1.5457, 0.7442, 0.7442]   ⛔ MAX 1.5457 > 1.35 — the
                                                                 DELETED pin REDS here
pairwise odds ratios     = 2.0769 (deploy vs each other move)
                           0.4815 (each other move vs deploy)
                           1.0000 (between any two floored moves)
                         ⭐ range [0.4815, 2.0769] = EXACTLY [0.65/1.35, 1.35/0.65]
```

**THE SHARPEST REACHABLE CASE — the reinforced move is the LOW-probability one** (same
scores, factors `[0.65, 0.65, 0.65, 1.35]`): per-probability ratios
`[0.9659, 0.9659, 0.9659, 2.0061]`, i.e. **2.0061 — 49% past the deleted pin's cap**;
odds-ratio range unchanged at `[0.4815, 2.0769]`. **THE FULL ELEVEN-MOVE COURT — ⭐⭐ NOW
STATED WITH ITS SCORE SET, WHICH IS WHY THE FIGURE MOVED.** ⚠⚠ **THE ⛔ STRUCK SPELLING
WAS 1.7941 AND IT DOES NOT REPRODUCE:** it was published without naming the scores it was
computed from, so nobody could re-execute it — **the exact defect the EXECUTED-TABLE LAW
exists to stop, committed one paragraph above the table that states the law.** RE-EXECUTED
against the PRESERVED sweep script (**`VERIFY_oddsratio.py`, md5
`ec974edb4ee8f0b776d77d887f28bc6e`**, case C — ⭐ **THE HASH IS DERIVED BY `countsweep.py`
FROM THE FILE ON DISK AND DIFFED AGAINST THIS SENTENCE LIKE ANY OTHER COUNTED QUANTITY**,
because figures are only re-checkable if a reader can tell WHICH script produced them;
⚠ it moved at round four when the case-C comment was corrected — the comment had named the
⛔ STRUCK `1.7941` as the script's TARGET, which is an instruction to a future reader to
"fix" the script until a figure that does not reproduce comes back), whose score set is
**`[0.90] + [0.62] × 10`** with factors `[1.35] + [0.65] × 10` at `STRATEGY_K = 3.5`,
giving `p_deploy = 0.2104` dark: **`deploy`'s per-probability ratio is `1.6933`**;
odds-ratio range **exactly `[0.4815, 2.0769]`** again — the bound does not widen with the
candidate count, which is the property that makes it a law rather than a curve fit.
⭐ **THE REFUTATION IS UNCHANGED AND DOES NOT DEPEND ON WHICH FIGURE IS RIGHT: BOTH
`1.6933` AND THE STRUCK `1.7941` EXCEED THE DELETED PIN'S `1.35` CAP**, as do the two
hand-walked four-move cases (`1.5457`, `2.0061`). A single reachable violating state is
dispositive, and there are four of them — **so the arithmetic that killed the
per-probability pin stands exactly as it did, and what changed is only that the
eleven-move case can now be re-run by a reader.**

**THE RANDOMIZED SWEEP, EXECUTED — 200,000 reachable `(score, factor)` states with
`n ∈ {2, 3, 4, 5, 11}`. ⚠⚠ A TABLE HEADED *EXECUTED* CARRIES ONLY FIGURES THAT
REPRODUCE, and one row of it did not:**

| Pin | Result |
|---|---|
| **DELETED** — per-probability inside `(0.65, 1.35)` | ⛔ violation rate **0.2643** — the preserved sweep script re-executed at its own seed — corroborated at **0.2645** by an independent re-implementation on a different seed. Worst observed **1.9380**, which reproduces EXACTLY. **UNSATISFIABLE**, and the refutation does not depend on the rate at all |
| ⭐ **THE LAW** — pairwise odds ratio inside `[0.4815, 2.0769]` | observed range **[0.481791, 2.075591]** against the analytic **[0.481481, 2.076923]** — **ZERO violations**, and the observed range fills the analytic one, which is the reachability half of the same measurement |

⚠⚠ **WHY THE VIOLATION RATE MOVED, AND WHICH FIGURES IN THIS TABLE ARE LOAD-BEARING.**
The polish published **0.1756** and it does not reproduce: re-executing the preserved
sweep at its own seed returns **0.2643**, and an independent re-implementation on a
different seed returns **0.2645** — two measurements that agree with each other and not
with the published one, so the published figure is STRUCK rather than defended. **A
violation RATE is a property of the SAMPLING DISTRIBUTION** — the score and factor
draws, the mix of candidate counts `n`, and whether a state that violates once is
counted once or per-candidate — so it is the one number in this table that is an
artefact of how the sweep was built rather than of the law under test. ⭐ **Everything
the refutation actually rests on reproduces EXACTLY: the analytic interval
`[(1−s)/(1+s), (1+s)/(1−s)] = [0.481481, 2.076923]`, the worst observed per-probability
ratio `1.9380`, the hand-walked anchor cases (`1.5457`, `2.0061`), and ZERO odds-ratio
violations.** The deleted pin is unsatisfiable because a single reachable state exceeds
it — the four hand-walked cases above each do — and that argument never needed a rate.
⚠ The observed odds-ratio EXTREMES are sampling figures too and are restated at their
re-executed values; only their sixth digits moved, and the reachability conclusion (the
observed range fills the analytic one) is unchanged.

⚠ **THE PIN IS THE ODDS RATIO AT EVERY SITE CLASS, WITH THAT SITE'S OWN CONVERSION
STATED** — §3c's application-law table carries the per-idiom column, and the keyed race
and the argmin state theirs in their own terms (a keyed race's odds ratio is the
win-share odds ratio over the hash population; an argmin's is the flip-rate odds between
two candidates). ⭐ **AND THE SECOND HALF OF THE LAW IS NOT DECORATION:** the "never 0,
never 1" clause is what the odds ratio alone does not give — an odds ratio is
scale-free and would tolerate a candidate at probability `1e-9`. Both halves are
asserted, and the FLOOR/CAP asymmetry fence (`NEUTRAL_I - FLOOR_I === CAP_I - NEUTRAL_I`)
is what makes the second half structural rather than checked.

**WHAT THE OLD PARAGRAPH GOT RIGHT AND KEEPS.** The reasoning that moved the load
POST-SOFTMAX is untouched and is re-affirmed here, because it is what makes ANY bounded
law available at this site:

The compile pinned the factor
("strictly inside `(1 - HABIT_SPAN, 1 + HABIT_SPAN)`") and reasoned about the factor,
and at a softmax site that pin **bounds a quantity that does not bound the behaviour the
law is about.** MEASURED: `contestMath.js#softmaxWeights` computes `const scaled =
scores.map((s) => k * s); … const exps = scaled.map((s) => Math.exp(s - max));`, and
`settlementStrategy.js` calls it with `STRATEGY_K = 3.5`. Multiplying a score `s` by a
factor `f` before that call yields `exp(3.5·s·f) = (exp(3.5·s))^f` — **f is an EXPONENT
on the un-normalised weight, which is the very thing §3c's exponent prohibition
forbids, arrived at by a different door.** Worked on the compile's own bounds: two moves
at the `clamp01` ceiling `s = 1.0`, one at `f = 1.35` and one at `f = 0.65`, give a
weight ratio of `exp(3.5 · 0.70) = 11.6` — **92%/8% out of a 50/50 base**; even the
sustainable case (one move at cap, rivals decayed to neutral) gives `exp(3.5 · 0.35) =
3.4×`. The second softmax consumer is worse in kind: `contestOverThirdParty.js` computes
`const logits = cleaned.map((c) => logit(c.scoreFor)); const base =
softmaxWeights(logits, opt.temperature);` — **a multiplicand on a LOGIT scales
log-odds, which is exponentiation of the odds.** So:

> ⛔ **THE PIN THIS REPLACES, RECORDED SO NOBODY RE-DERIVES IT:** *"the ratio
> `p_habit(move) / p_dark(move)` lies strictly inside `(1 - HABIT_SPAN, 1 +
> HABIT_SPAN)` for every move"*. **DELETED AS FALSE (R16 / P1).** The quantity is
> `f_i / mean_f` and its true range is `[(1−s)/(1+s), (1+s)/(1−s)]`. The FACTOR pin
> survives as a UNIT test on `habitFactor` — `f ∈ (1−s, 1+s)`, which is true and
> checkable — and the ODDS-RATIO pin above is the law's evidence.

The per-idiom conversion that makes the odds-ratio pin satisfiable is §3c's
**PER-SITE-CLASS APPLICATION LAW** — at a softmax the load applies to the POST-softmax
probability and the vector is renormalized, which makes the ODDS ratio exactly the FACTOR
ratio (the renormalizer cancels), bounded by construction. **Pinning the odds ratio and
applying post-softmax are one ruling stated twice**: the application law is what makes the
measurement true, and the measurement is what proves the application law was obeyed.
⚠ **AND THE MEASUREMENT NOW HAS A GUARD-THE-GUARD, because this is the second time this
law has been stated wrong:** the odds-ratio pin ships with a PLANTED PRE-SOFTMAX
multiplicand as its mutant (which drives the odds ratio to `exp(k·s·Δ)` and reds
loudly) **and** with a reachability assertion that the observed range FILLS the analytic
one — a bound nothing ever approaches is a bound nobody has tested.

⭐⭐ **CONSERVATIVE REINFORCEMENT — THE SATURATION ASYMMETRY, RULED AS DESIGN AND NAMED
(the H2 closure, J-HB-15).** The compile's curve is symmetric around `NEUTRAL_I` and
assumed reinforce and decay are mirror operations at every site. **At a saturating
idiom they are not, and the estate's saturating idiom is everywhere.** MEASURED:
`settlementStrategy.js` applies every existing load as `clamp01(scored[move] * mult)` —
the coalition load, the commitment load, and (as a division) the disposition bar — and
the moves are already clamped at emission (`scored.defend = clamp01(O.defend.base +
(ctx.homeBesieged ? O.defend.besiegedBonus : 0) + …)` under the in-source comment
"defend — always legal. Strong when besieged or when the settlement is weak."). **A
besieged settlement's `defend` score sits at or near the ceiling — and `siege_pressed`
is the first circumstance class this volume names.** A factor above 1 on a saturated
score is INERT; a factor below 1 always bites.

Two things close it, and both are stated rather than one being left to imply the other:

1. **AT SOFTMAX SITES THE ASYMMETRY IS REMOVED, NOT TOLERATED.** The application law
   (§3c) puts the load on the POST-softmax probability, downstream of every `clamp01`.
   Site 7 — the anchor, and the site where the asymmetry was sharpest — no longer meets
   a clamp on the habit path at all.
2. **WHERE AN IDIOM GENUINELY SATURATES (the keyed race's `entry.weight`, an EV argmax
   over `clamp01` values), THE ASYMMETRY IS THE RULING:**

> **CONSERVATIVE REINFORCEMENT. Habit may never push an option past certainty; it may
> only drag one back from it. Where a candidate's own score is already saturated,
> reinforcement is INERT and decay is LIVE — by design, because a court that has
> already concluded a move is the only thing to do is not made more certain by having
> done it before, while a court that has been burned doing it can still hesitate.**

⚠ **THE ASYMMETRY IS DECLARED, MEASURED, AND BUDGETED — never silent.** HB-4 and HB-5
each record, per saturating site, **the measured share of decisions in which the
reinforcement arm was inert** on a generated corpus, in the module header. If that share
is high at a site, the site's own scoring is saturated and the finding belongs to that
site's owner, not to the habit curve — and it is a chair report, not a habit tuning
row. ⛔ **THE FORBIDDEN FIX: un-clamping a foreign score to make room for habit.** That
is a behaviour change to a chooser this program does not own, it moves dark-world bytes,
and it is exactly what J-HB-6 refuses in the guard-chain case.

**REFINEMENT 16, AND THE ONE COUNTERINTUITIVE ROW.** `SEVERITY_W` is NOT monotone:
`ruinous` (0.80) teaches LESS than `grave` (1.40). This is deliberate and it is
refinement 12's own words ("mixed/ruinous outcomes teach less"). The reasoning, recorded
so nobody "fixes" it: **a ruinous outcome is more often circumstance than choice.** A
court annihilated after choosing to hold did not learn that holding is bad; it learned
that it was outmatched. Over-fitting to catastrophe is precisely the variance collapse
§0.4 exists to prevent, and it is the one place where the curve itself — rather than the
information war — carries a corrective. J-HB-7 records it as vetoable.

⭐ **REFINEMENT 12 IS TOTAL, NOT ADVISORY — the severity path has no ungraded road.**
The directive rules that grades come ONLY from existing outcome vocabularies and that
the severity ladder SCALES the update. Made total, that is three assertions and they
are walker-enforced together: (i) **every closing vocabulary maps TOTALLY into
`SEVERITY_LADDER ∪ {LAPSE}`** (the grade-map totality law, §3a) — MEASURED,
`bandFamilies.js` exports `SEVERITY_LADDER = Object.freeze(['glancing', 'telling',
'grave', 'ruinous'])` and has ZERO external consumers today, so HB is its first and the
spelling is inherited rather than chosen; (ii) **`SEVERITY_W` has an entry for all four
rungs** — a missing rung would default to `undefined` and silently zero a step, so the
map is asserted key-equal to the ladder itself; (iii) **no habit module authors a
severity word**, which half two of `spBandFamilies.walker` already enforces estate-wide.
⚠ The consequence worth stating: **there is no "ungraded reinforcement" road.** A close
either produces a rung (and the update is scaled) or produces `LAPSE` (and nothing is
written). "It happened but we do not know how well" is not a representable outcome, and
that is deliberate — it is where credit-assignment creep would enter.

⭐⭐ **REFINEMENT 16'S SECOND HALF — THE INTELLIGENCE SHELF LIFE, REBUILT ON THE
BELIEVED CLOCK (the P4 closure). THE TRUTH-CLOCK VERSION IS STRUCK.**

**WHAT THE AMENDMENT CLAIMED AND WHY IT COULD NOT BE BUILT.** It asserted that *"believed
doctrine decays on the TARGET'S modulated clock, not the observer's"* and that *"no new
machinery buys this — it falls out of one shared band lookup"*, while §3d simultaneously
bound J-SP-1's *"no second decay law"*. **MEASURED, those two cannot both be true, three
ways:** (i) the belief family decays **CONFIDENCE, never the believed VALUE** — MEASURED,
`beliefMap.js#reconcileBelief` on silence returns `prior ? { ...prior, confidence01:
round4(priorConf) } : …` under the in-source note *"Silence: keep the (frozen) prior
value"*, and `beliefAxisSubjects.js#foldSubjectAxes` holds a below-bar band verbatim
(`const next = adopt || held === undefined ? truth : held;`) — **so a stolen sheet does
not go stale, it goes UNCERTAIN**; (ii) the decay base is ONE global constant
(`BELIEF_TUNING.SILENCE_DECAY: 0.92`) with **no per-TARGET parameter at all**, so keying
it on the subject's law word means editing the shared decay for all three existing SP-B
families — a behaviour change and a golden shift — or minting the second decay law the
volume forbids; (iii) it counts `silentTicks`, not weeks
(`export function decayedConfidence(confidence01, silentTicks, decayKeep01 = 0)`), so the
units trap J-HB-17 dissolved for the habit stock would reappear on the belief side.

⭐⭐ **THE CHAIR'S RULING (P4), AND IT IS MORE COHESIVE THAN THE THING IT REPLACES.
BELIEVED DOCTRINE DECAYS ON THE OBSERVER'S *BELIEVED* LAW BAND OF THE TARGET.** You do
not discount intelligence by how volatile the target ACTUALLY is — you cannot know that.
**You discount it by how volatile you BELIEVE the target to be.** Three things fall out,
and the third is a gift:

1. **IT IS BELIEF-ROUTED BY CONSTRUCTION**, which the truth-clock version never was. The
   truth version had an observer reading a target's real alignment axis to price its own
   intelligence — an omniscience leak of exactly the shape §3f names and closes elsewhere
   in this volume. The believed version reads the observer's own row and nothing else.
2. ⭐ **THE CARRIER EXISTS AND IS PER-PAIR ALREADY, SO "NO NEW MACHINERY" BECOMES TRUE
   INSTEAD OF ASPIRATIONAL.** MEASURED, `beliefMap.js`:
   `function adjustedDecayBase(decayKeep01) { const k = clamp(finiteNumber(decayKeep01,
   0), -1, 1); const base = BELIEF_TUNING.SILENCE_DECAY; if (k === 0) return base; return
   k > 0 ? base + (1 - base) * k : base * (1 + k); }` — a **signed per-PAIR modifier,
   documented as *"the W-DOCTRINE-2b SEE/HIDE decay modifier"*, threaded through
   `decayedConfidence(confidence01, silentTicks, decayKeep01 = 0)` with `0 ⇒ EXACTLY
   BELIEF_TUNING.SILENCE_DECAY (the byte-identity anchor)`.** The observer is one half of
   that pair. **A believed-volatility term composes INTO `decayKeep01` as a second signed
   contribution** — negative for a believed-`lawless` subject (decay FASTER), zero for
   `balanced`, positive for `lawful` (decay SLOWER) — and the composition is bounded by
   the existing `clamp(k, -1, 1)`. **No per-target parameter is added, no shared constant
   is edited, no second decay law is minted, the three existing SP-B families are
   untouched, and absent ⇒ 0 ⇒ byte-identical through the door that already exists.**
3. ⭐ **THE CURRENCY IS CONFIDENCE, WHICH IS THE HONEST ONE.** Because the belief family
   decays confidence and freezes value, the shelf life is expressed as *"this reading is
   old and you should not trust it"* rather than as a value silently drifting toward
   neutral. **That is a better model of intelligence than the one it replaces:** a spy's
   sheet does not slowly turn into a different sheet, it stays exactly what it said and
   becomes less worth acting on. The `hold` rung and the belief `confidence01` are the
   two things the dossier already prints, so the surface is built.

**WHERE THE BELIEVED LAW BAND LIVES — one more key inside the family HB-6 is already
minting, never a fifth family.** `doctrineBands` gains a `law` member beside its
per-class sheet:

```
doctrineBands: {
  law:   <a LAW_WORDS member — the observer's BELIEVED law band of the subject>,
  sheet: { <circumstanceClass>: { move: <actionToken>, hold: <holdRung> } }
}
```

Both halves ride the SAME arm, the SAME adoption bar (`DOCTRINE_ADOPT`), the SAME
drop-when-absent rule, and the SAME `bestFidelity` gate — so a court that has never had a
clear-eyed look at a subject has NO believed law band and its `decayKeep01` contribution
is exactly 0. The truth side is one existing call: MEASURED,
`lawWord.js#lawWordFor(lawfulness01)` over `LAW_WORDS = Object.freeze(['balanced',
'lawful', 'lawless'])`, and the axis is
`settlementAlignment.js#settlementAlignment(item, worldState)` → `{ lawfulness01,
malice01, aggressiveness }` with the in-source contract *"`lawfulness01` — 0 lawless … 1
lawful-bureaucratic; EXACTLY 0.5 = no signal"*. **No new derivation, no new vocabulary,
no new truth door — `axisGroundTruth` is still THE ONE.**

⭐⭐ **AND HERE IS WHAT THE TRUTH-CLOCK VERSION COULD NEVER HAVE DONE: SHELF LIFE ITSELF
BECOMES DECEIVABLE.** Because the discount is keyed on a BELIEF, it is a belief a liar can
plant. **A court that cultivates a chaotic image devalues every piece of intelligence
anyone holds about it** — including intelligence that is completely accurate. It does not
have to change what it does; it only has to change what others believe about how steady it
is, and their own sheets rot in their hands. Conversely, **a court that plants a LAWFUL
image makes stale intelligence about itself feel durable**, which is the sharper attack:
the mark keeps acting on a sheet it has stopped checking. ⚠ **RECORD THIS AS A DESIGNED
CONSEQUENCE, NOT A SIDE EFFECT** — it is §0.4's self-balancing loop reaching a surface the
compile did not know it had, and it is the first mechanism in this estate where the
INFORMATION about information is itself a weapon. **The plant road that carries it is the
one HB-8 already builds** (a synthetic `BeliefReport` asserting a `doctrineBands` value —
now including its `law` member — with the `plant:*` disinfo lineage row), so there is no
fourth road and no new commission shape.

⚠ **THE ONE THING THIS DOES NOT BUY, said plainly:** the target's OWN learning rate and
half-life still key on the target's TRUE law word (§3b's curve), because that is the
target's own memory and it is not a belief about anything. **The two clocks are
deliberately different and the difference is the drama:** the truth clock governs how fast
a court actually learns and forgets; the believed clock governs how fast an observer's
picture of that court loses credibility. A court whose true and believed law bands
disagree is exactly the court whose doctrine sheet is worth stealing and dangerous to
trust — and the dossier's divergence line already has the grammar to say so.

**THE LAW WORD IS READ ONCE, FROM THE ONE HOME.** `lawWordFor(lawfulness01)` in
`src/domain/worldPulse/lawWord.js` — MEASURED at HEAD, exporting
`LAW_WORDS = ['balanced','lawful','lawless']` and `LAW_WORD_EDGES`. This is the CR-ES-3
unified vocabulary and there is no second spelling to pick. ⚠ The array is CODEPOINT-
SORTED; the volatility ORDER (lawless → balanced → lawful) is semantic and lives in the
tuning maps, never in an index.

**THE SEAT SHOCK (refinement 8), and the read/write agreement trap it creates.** A
change of the deciding seat applies a ONE-TIME shock toward neutral: the new seat has
yet to learn the old lessons, but it inherits a court, its clerks, and its habits of
mind — so a partial keep, never a reset and never a carry-forward. ⚠⚠ **THE TRAP: a
lazy read that applies the shock is a HIDDEN WRITE, and a write that applies it while
the read does not makes the read and the write disagree about what the stock is.** THE
CURE, and it is one line of architecture: `seatAdjusted(stock, entrySeat, currentSeat)`
is a PURE function with exactly TWO callers — the read path and the write path — and
both call it. The read applies it arithmetically without persisting; the write applies
it and re-stamps `seat`. Because the function is idempotent once the stamp is refreshed,
the two never disagree. The pin is a differential: the factor computed on an unwritten
world equals the factor computed after the next write, for every seeded seat change.
⚠ The seat identity resolves through the ONE existing resolver, **never `.name`** — the
FACTION-KEY DEFECT CLASS has bitten four times. VERIFY-AT-BUILD that a stable seat id
exists; **if it does not, the seat-shock arm ships DARK-DECLARED rather than keyed on an
unstable value.**

**⚠⚠ THE ROUNDING FENCE — the one real threat to replay, named and fenced.**
`decayTowardNeutral` computes `Math.pow(0.5, age / weeks)`, and `Math.pow` is
implementation-approximated per the ECMAScript spec. A ULP difference across engines
could, at a boundary, flip a comparison and therefore a choice. THREE THINGS ARE TRUE
and all three are stated:

1. **The estate already carries this exposure.** `dispositionLedger.decayedStock01`,
   `momentum.decayedCommitmentStock`, `relationshipMemory`, and twelve more sites all
   compute the same `Math.pow(0.5, …)` today. Habit introduces no NEW class of risk.
2. **Habit AMPLIFIES it, because the value feeds a SELECTION.** A disposition
   multiplier that differs by 2e-17 shifts a score by 2e-17; a habit factor that
   differs by 2e-17 can, at a knife-edge, select a different move and fork the world.
   That difference in consequence is why the fence exists.
3. **THE FENCE: the stock is stored and compared as an INTEGER in 1e-4 units, and every
   factor is derived from the ROUNDED integer, never from the float.** Rounding happens
   BEFORE any comparison, on every path, so a ULP difference is annihilated unless the
   true value sits exactly on a 5e-5 boundary — a measure-zero set, and identically
   located on every device because the rounding is the same operation everywhere.
   `roundToUnits` is the single rounding door; a float stock never leaves the curve leaf.

The residual is recorded honestly rather than waved away, and the fuller cure —
integer-domain decay through a precomputed rational half-life table — is chair
question **Q3** with a recommendation to defer.

**THE TIMING LAW, copied verbatim from the ledger that already learns.**
`dispositionLedger` states it in-source: *callers READ last-tick ledger at
candidate-build and WRITE next-tick post-apply, so a contest that resolves on tick N
only colours behaviour from tick N+1 — never a half-updated mid-tick read.* Habit
adopts this without amendment. **A habit credited at tick N is invisible to every
chooser until tick N+1.** ⛔ **A design that re-derives `habitStrength` by scanning
`pulseHistory` BREAKS THE PROMISE** and is forbidden in writing: `MAX_HISTORY = 80`,
`collapseIntervalHistory` destroys N-1 interior records, and the war-convergence
collector's own receipt records the resulting 1-in-52 loss of termination reads. The
stream is a fine TRIGGER (a close happened this tick) and a fatal LEDGER.

⭐⭐ **THE BANDS LAW, REWRITTEN AGAINST THE REAL CONVENTION (the P8 closure). THE §7
SPELLING IS STRUCK AS INCOHERENT.**

**WHAT WAS WRONG, stated at its source.** The volume claimed twice — here and at §8.2 —

over the whole volume returns exactly ONE hit (HB-0's); HB-1 through HB-9 have none. **A

> ⭐⭐ **THE BANDS LAW (J-HB-21). A `**Bands:**` line is carried by TUNING-BEARING WAVES

⚠⚠ **THE ANCHORED-EXTRACTOR CLASS, NAMED, BECAUSE THIS VOLUME JUST DEMONSTRATED IT ON
ITSELF (M4).** The walker's instrument is the START-OF-LINE regex `^\*\*Bands:\*\*`, and
**it stays anchored**: an unanchored scan matches every prose mention of the token —
this section alone quotes it five times inside backticks — so dropping the anchor would
trade a silent miss for a corpus of false positives and a walker nobody trusts. ⛔ **The
price of the anchor is that ANY prefix before the token makes the line invisible, and
that is exactly the hole that opened here: HB-6's line was authored as `⭐ **Bands:** …`
and the glyph put it outside the anchor, so a ten-wave corpus scanned 9/10 while reading
as complete to every human eye.** ⭐ **THE CURE IS A SPELLING RULE, NOT A LOOSER REGEX:
the `**Bands:**` token starts the line, and decoration goes AFTER it** — HB-6 is
re-spelled accordingly and the scan now returns **10 of 10**. ⚠ The walker owes the
matching assertion: the anchored hit count EQUALS the wave count, so a re-decorated line
reds instead of vanishing. **A scan whose miss looks identical to a clean pass is not an
instrument; the count assertion is what makes it one.**

⚠ `SOAK_COUPLINGS` is a
closed six-member vocabulary; a habit band would need a SEVENTH member and therefore a
