## §6 JUDGMENT BLOCKS (every one vetoable; an implementer NEVER re-rules one silently)

**J-HB-1 — THE HOST.** Habit state lives in its OWN `spatialLedgers` sub-key, not as a
dimension on `dispositionStats`. Full reasoning at §0.5. **Veto cost:**
`habitConditioningEnabled` becomes unusable without `dispositionChannelsEnabled`, and
the habit stock inherits `dispositionLedger`'s hand-rolled decay as a sixteenth fork.

**J-HB-2 — THE DECIDING IDENTITY.** Institutional by default with a seat stamp and a
seat-change decay shock; per-NPC habits ONLY where per-NPC decision machinery already
exists (measured: the errand/espionage person choices and `npcLadderGoals`). **Veto
cost:** a roster-grain ledger is two orders of magnitude larger and lands in
`npcLadderState`, an already-busy persisted surface.

**J-HB-3 — THE DECAY LAW.** HB becomes `bandedStock.decayTowardNeutral`'s FIRST `src`
rider. It does NOT hand-roll a sixteenth `Math.pow(0.5, …)`, and it does NOT migrate
`dispositionLedger` onto the family as a precondition. **The reasoning for NOT migrating:**
that is a behaviour-preserving refactor of a flagged-dark ledger read by sixteen modules,
and bundling it into a habit wave would make a habit red indistinguishable from a
disposition red. **Recorded as a nameable future wave**, not as debt this program hides.

**J-HB-4 — NEVER KEY ON IDENTITY.** Habits key on typed circumstance classes and typed
actions only, never on counterpart identity. Reasoning at §1.3. **Veto cost:** unbounded
per-actor ledger growth, an unnameable prose surface, and a doctrine sheet that is a
database rather than a sheet.

**J-HB-5 — THE PLEDGE BOOK CARRIES THE CREDIT JOIN, AND NO FOREIGN RECORD CHANGES SHAPE.
⭐⭐ THE TITLE IS CORRECTED AT ROUND FOUR: THE OLD ONE — "NOT FOREIGN OPENING STAMPS" — IS
FALSE AS AN ABSOLUTE, BECAUSE THIS PROGRAM DOES WRITE TWO OF THEM.** The credit join
itself lives in HB's own pledge book and nothing foreign holds it; what rides outward is
two stamps, not a join.
⛔⛔ **THE PREMISE THAT STOOD HERE IS STRUCK AT SOURCE — SEE J-HB-13.** It read: *"keyed by
an episode key both sides compute independently."* That is R4's refuted claim restated in
J-HB-5's own words, and it outlived its own refutation by three review rounds — the
register killed it in §0.3 while the ruling that depends on it went on asserting it.
**RULED (J-HB-13): the episode key is a VALUE MINTED AT OPEN AND CARRIED**, so two
records receive an additive stamp — `deployment.habitEpisode` and `treaty.habitEpisode`
— and no site computes a key twice. ⭐ **THIS IS THE INSTANCE THAT MINTED THE
STRUCK-PREMISE SCAN (J-HB-28):** a stale CLAIM is the same defect as a stale COUNT one
level up, and the count ledger was structurally blind to it.
⭐ **THE SURVIVING CLAIM, WHICH IS WHAT THIS RULING IS ACTUALLY FOR, AND IT IS NARROWER
THAN THE TITLE WAS:** the two carried fields are **additive drop-when-absent fields on
records the estate already persists**, written only under `habitConditioningEnabled` —
**not** the `occupation` / `tradeWarState` / coup-verdict schema moves census 2's H-3
believed were owed. No foreign record changes SHAPE; two already-persisted records gain
one optional field each, and R4's surviving half stands exactly as §0.3 states it.
**Veto cost — RE-PRICED AT ROUND FOUR, because the old figure was arithmetic on a premise
that had already fallen.** ⛔ The struck spelling read: *"three owner-gated schema moves on
live campaign state, to buy exactly what the pledge book buys for free."* ⚠⚠ **THE PLEDGE
BOOK HAS NOT BEEN FREE SINCE J-HB-13, AND ITS PRICE IS Q1** — two owner-gated stamped
fields, put to the owner as two. The three schema moves were never the alternative on
offer either, because the pledge book discharges the H-3 obligation whether or not Q1 is
signed. What a veto of J-HB-5 costs is the credit join itself: with no pledge book there
is no episode ledger for ANY close to grade against, and the alternative becomes a
persisted-shape change on every lifecycle record whose close needs grading — which is
where census 2's three came from before the pledge book replaced them. **See Q1 for the
live price and J-HB-13 for the road.**

**J-HB-6 — GUARD CHAINS STAY DETERMINISTIC.** `razingGate`, both `ransomChoices` arms,
and `deliberationRead` do not join. Adding a weighted arm to a pure guard chain is a
BEHAVIOUR CHANGE, not a habit input: it moves dark-world bytes and breaks the four-fence
contract. An alignment gate is legality, not preference. **Veto cost:** each conversion
becomes an individually owner-signed new capability with its own golden shift.

**J-HB-7 — `ruinous` TEACHES LESS THAN `grave`.** `SEVERITY_W` is deliberately
non-monotone. A ruinous outcome is more often circumstance than choice, and over-fitting
to catastrophe is the variance collapse the design exists to prevent. **Veto cost:** a
monotone ladder is simpler to explain and makes annihilation the loudest teacher in the
world.

**J-HB-8 — THE SHEET SPEAKS RUNGS, NEVER COUNTS.** The directive's illustrative "as it
has thrice before" is realized as a firmness phrase, not a tally. Reasoning at §3g.
**Veto cost, RE-GROUNDED at the amendment (R8):** the underlying stock is a DECAYED
CONTINUOUS VALUE whose "count" nobody can honestly print — that is the whole of the
argument and it stands alone. ⚠ The compile's second reason ("a live `proseNumerics`
scanner that is already red at base") is **STRUCK: the scanner is GREEN at 413** and the
ruled re-record landed at CR-FP-2. A veto here costs a prose surface that would have to
invent an integer the model does not have; it no longer costs anything to do with a red.

**J-HB-9 — TRACKED, NOT EXEMPT.** `habits` is classified TRACKED in `spatialUsage.js`,
against census 4's tentative EXEMPT. The EXEMPT criterion is "adoption is legible from
the flag", and that fails for a virtual flag lit in no preset — which is exactly why
`routeNetwork` and `demographicPlans` are TRACKED. **Veto cost:** a ledger whose adoption
nothing reports.

**J-HB-10 — THE SHEET IS MODAL, NOT DISTRIBUTIONAL.** One move and one firmness rung per
class. **Veto cost:** a distribution is a float surface — unprintable under L5,
unpinnable under the no-decimal runtime rule, and unstealable as a narrative object.

**J-HB-11 — THE DAMPER MINTS NO CANDIDATE.** The self-balancing loop narrows the actor's
own span rather than adding a "deviate" move to every vocabulary. **Veto cost:** **one
additional move** at every joined site, each owing a close, a prose pool, and a
reachability measurement. ⚠ **RE-SPELLED AT ROUND FOUR, AND THE OLD SPELLING IS WORTH
NAMING BECAUSE IT WAS ANCHORED TO NOTHING.** All three homes of this argument said
"thirteenth" — but the largest action vocabulary any joined site carries is **ELEVEN**
(`STRATEGY_MOVES`, site 7), and no vocabulary in this volume has twelve members. The
thirteen was borrowed from the **TWELVE CIRCUMSTANCE CLASSES**, which are the *keys* a
habit is filed under and not options anybody chooses between — so the number was
arithmetic performed on the wrong table. The argument never needed a numeral: adding ONE
move to every vocabulary is the cost, whatever each vocabulary's size.

**J-HB-12 — THE ROUNDING FENCE.** Stocks are stored and compared as integers in 1e-4
units; every factor derives from the rounded integer. **Veto cost:** the estate's
existing `Math.pow` exposure, which today only shifts a score, would be able to fork a
selection — see Q3.

---

**THE FIVE AMENDMENT RULINGS (J-HB-13..J-HB-17), each vetoable, each answering a
finding the cohesion pass proved by execution.**

**J-HB-13 — THE EPISODE KEY IS MINTED AT OPEN AND CARRIED.** A key computed
independently at two sites is a coincidence maintained by hand; a key carried is a fact.
⭐ **The EIGHT closes are audited into CARRIED {1, 4, 6, 8}, RECOMPUTED {3, 7}, ⭐ BOTH
ROADS {2}, and one declared VERIFY-AT-BUILD {5} (§3a) — close 8 added at the polish
(R18), and it carries on a record the estate already persists, so it costs nothing new.**
⭐⭐ **CLOSE 2's BOTH-ROADS BUCKET IS MINTED AT THE ATTESTATION:** it is RECOMPUTED for
`warPeaceDecision`'s accept/refuse episode (`terms:<treatyId>`) and CARRIED for
`settlementStrategy`'s keyed sue episode (`treaty.habitEpisode`), **and each road takes
its own pin** — the flat "RECOMPUTED {2, 3, 7}" spelling described only the first and
would have shipped the sue join with no carrier. Close 1 carries through
the warIntent order onto `deployment.habitEpisode`; ⭐ **the `sue_for_peace` arm carries on
the same road onto `treaty.habitEpisode` (P3) — ⚠ CONDITIONALLY, IFF `bilateralOffer`
(J-HB-24), because the three values the key is built from are emitted behind that gate and
nowhere else.** **Veto cost:** close 1 cannot recompute
its key from either side — MEASURED, the chooser runs a tick before the deployment exists
and the close's delta carries `{id, outcome, magnitude}` only — so vetoing the carry does
not restore recomputation, it DELETES the war close. The war chooser would then read a
stock nothing writes **on the ONE move that has a close at all** (R17/R18), which is the
whole of site 7's coverage. See Q1, now two fields.

**J-HB-14 — THE HABIT LOAD APPLIES POST-SOFTMAX.** At a softmax site a multiplicand on
the score is an exponent on the weight (`exp(k·s·f) = (exp(k·s))^f`), so the compile's own
exponent prohibition was breached in substance by its own idiom table. The load therefore
multiplies the POST-softmax probability and renormalizes. **Veto cost:** at
`STRATEGY_K = 3.5` and the stated span, two saturated moves go from 50/50 to **92/8** — a
tilt that is a lock in everything but name. ⚠ **NARROWED AT THE POLISH: this ruling is now
about the APPLICATION POINT ONLY.** Its second half — *"and tilt-never-lock is measured as
a probability ratio"* — is SUPERSEDED by J-HB-18, because that measurement is
mathematically impossible at the stated bound. **The application ruling was right and the
measurement ruling was wrong, and they are separated here so a veto of one cannot be read
as a veto of the other.**

**J-HB-15 — CONSERVATIVE REINFORCEMENT (the saturation asymmetry, RULED as design).**
Where a candidate's own score is already saturated, reinforcement is INERT and decay is
LIVE. Habit may never push an option past certainty; it may only drag one back from it.
The asymmetry is NAMED, MEASURED per site, and reported — never silent. **Veto cost:**
the alternative is un-clamping a foreign scorer to make room for habit, which is a
behaviour change to a chooser this program does not own and moves dark-world bytes.

**J-HB-16 — A REFUTED SITE TAKES A DEAD-CODE ROW AND A CHAIR REPORT, NEVER A DELETE.**
`convergence.reactiveResponse` is measured forkless, worldless and uncalled in `src`,
with five live tests. It is struck from the site table and the seam table and recorded
as DEAD-CODE in the fork registry. **It is not deleted by this program.** **Veto cost:**
deleting foreign dead code inside a habit wave is unscoped scope, and the five tests
that exercise it are somebody's declared deferral until that module's own charter is
read. ⭐ **The generalization is the valuable half: `DEAD-CODE` is now a fourth
disposition in the registry**, so the next measured-uncalled fork has a place to be
recorded instead of being either joined or quietly removed.

**J-HB-17 — THE LEDGER STAMPS WEEKS, NEVER TICKS.** `worldState.calendar.elapsedWeeks`
is the authoritative integer campaign clock (MEASURED; the temporal constitution is
stated in `worldState.js` in-source), so `ageWeeks` is a subtraction of two integers and
no tick→week converter is minted, imported, or owed. ⭐ **SCOPED PRECISELY (the LOW-5
repair, §8.1 row 13): this is an AGE ruling.** It governs every quantity HB subtracts,
decays or compares against time. **It does NOT govern episode KEYS, whose tick components
are opaque IDENTITY COMPONENTS — compared only for equality, never converted and never
aged** — so `war:…:<orderTick>` and `terms:…:<peaceFrontSinceTick>` are compliant, not
exceptions. ⚠ The import-scan walker is the age half's instrument and cannot see the
identifier half; the key-shape pins hold that. **Veto cost:** the alternative the
compile left open was a tick-domain half-life — which is `dispositionLedger`'s
hand-rolled `Math.pow(0.5, age / CHANNEL_HALF_LIFE_TICKS)` under another name, i.e. the
SIXTEENTH fork that refinement 1 forbids, and it would silently mis-scale on any
interval but `one_week`.

---

**THE FIVE POLISH RULINGS (J-HB-18..J-HB-22), each vetoable, each answering a recohere
finding — and the first of them is the only ruling in this volume that was proved wrong
by ARITHMETIC rather than by a grep.**

**J-HB-18 — TILT-NEVER-LOCK IS AN INVARIANT ON PAIRWISE ODDS RATIOS, AND THE
PER-PROBABILITY CLAIM IS DELETED AS FALSE.** For any two candidates,
`(p'_i/p'_j)/(p_i/p_j) ∈ [(1−s)/(1+s), (1+s)/(1−s)]`, plus "never 0, never 1". This is
true BY CONSTRUCTION because the renormalizer cancels out of a ratio of ratios, leaving
exactly `f_i/f_j`. The amendment's claim — `p'_i/p_i` inside `(1∓s)` — is the quantity
`f_i/mean_f`, whose true range is the wider interval; EXECUTED, it is violated at rate
**0.2643 / 0.2645** (two independent re-executions — ⛔ the polish's **0.1756** is STRUCK
as non-reproducing) over 200,000 reachable states with a worst observed **1.9380**, which
DOES reproduce exactly. ⚠ **The ruling rests on the worst case and the analytic interval,
never on the rate:** a rate is a sampling-distribution artefact, and ONE reachable
violating state is already dispositive. It reds on the
exact state the design is built to produce (one action at cap, the rest at floor).
**Veto cost:** there is no per-candidate bound to fall back to. Vetoing the odds form
means either accepting the wider `[0.4815, 2.0769]` per-probability interval as the law
(which is the same law, stated in a form that varies with the candidate count and so
cannot be pinned once) or abandoning a bounded tilt guarantee entirely. ⚠ **The honest
warning attached to the veto: the pin this replaces was chartered into TWO waves as the
law's own proof, so the cost of leaving it standing is not a wrong sentence — it is two
waves that cannot go green.**

**J-HB-19 — ARITY IS PER-ACTION WHERE THE COUNTERPART EXISTS ONLY ON SOME ACTIONS, AND
`arity` IS NOT `anticipationWave`.** Site 7 is `arity: 'per-action'` with
`dyadicActions: ['deploy']` (MEASURED: both emission ternaries key on `move ===
'deploy'`). Separately, every joined row declares whether an anticipation term is
actually WIRED, so a **LEARN** row that is "dyadic" and that no wave wires ships the
ONE-TERM form with no second multiplicand in the expression at all (⭐ the `LEARN` clause
is J-HB-26's correction — a DEFER row ships no expression to be one-term). **Veto cost:** collapsing the two fields
back into one gives you either five sites carrying a multiplicand no wave makes ≠ 1 (an
undeclared dead arm at five seams) or five sites receiving a term outside HB-7's charter.
The amendment had both readings available and named neither.

**J-HB-20 — THE ACTION-COVERAGE LAW: A SITE JOINS ONLY WHERE AN ACTION HAS A CLOSE.**
Every action is either named in a close row or carries an explicit `NEEDS-A-CLOSE`
deferral row with a written `closeOwed`; a site whose actions are ALL closeless is DEFER,
not LEARN. This drops the joined set from twelve to six and grows the deferral book to
**FORTY-THREE rows (30 + 3 + 10)** — ⚠ the tenth per-action row is the UNKEYED
`sue_for_peace` path J-HB-24 later added, and the figure is re-parsed here rather than
inherited. **Veto cost:** the alternative is the state the volume was in — six
`LEARN` rows whose stocks nothing would ever write, including the anchor site's ten
un-closeable moves, i.e. dead arms at the exact places the design is loudest about. ⭐ The
law is also what would have caught the traditions row (a non-fork has no action vocabulary
to cover, so its set equality reds on an empty left-hand side), which is the argument for
it being a LAW rather than a table.

**J-HB-21 — THE BANDS LINE BELONGS TO TUNING-BEARING WAVES AND RECONCILES AGAINST §8.2.**
§7 is chair questions and is never a reconciliation target. Two waves bear tuning (HB-0,
HB-6); eight carry `**Bands:** NONE BY DESIGN` with the note naming what they author
instead; HB-9's envelopes are INSTRUMENT, not tuning. **Veto cost:** the amendment's
spelling made the HB-0 walker red against its own volume on day one, and put a deferral
book and a bands ledger in a section that holds five questions.

**J-HB-22 — BELIEVED DOCTRINE DECAYS ON THE OBSERVER'S BELIEVED LAW BAND OF THE TARGET,
THROUGH THE EXISTING PER-PAIR `decayKeep01`.** Not on the target's true clock — which
cannot be built without either editing `BELIEF_TUNING.SILENCE_DECAY` for all three
existing SP-B families or minting the second decay law J-SP-1 forbids, and which would
have an observer reading a target's real alignment axis to price its own intelligence.
⭐ **The believed form is more cohesive AND it makes shelf life deceivable**, which is a
capability the truth form did not have. **Veto cost:** reverting to the truth clock costs
a golden shift on three landed families, a second decay law, an omniscience leak, and the
deception surface — and it still would not work, because the belief family decays
CONFIDENCE and freezes VALUE, so there is no carrier for a value-staleness story at all.

---

**THE THREE ATTESTATION RULINGS (J-HB-23..J-HB-25), each vetoable, each answering an
attestation finding. ⚠⚠ ALL THREE ARE THE SAME DEFECT SHAPE IN THREE COSTUMES: a
HAND-MAINTAINED RESTATEMENT of something the document already contains, which went stale
when the thing it restated was edited.** A row count, a coverage verdict, and a set
membership — none of them was ever wrong at its source; each was wrong in a sentence that
had copied its source and then stopped watching it.

**J-HB-23 — THE NAMED-DOMAIN CHECKLIST ASSERTS THREE THINGS, AND ITS DENOMINATOR IS
FOURTEEN.** (a) row count `== 14`; (b) the label set `==` the CLOSED EXPLICIT EIGHT
`{ TRADE, FAITH, STRATEGY, ROUTE CREATION, ROUTE CHOICE, INTER-SETTLEMENT DYNAMICS,
GOALS, GOAL ORIENTATION }`, ⛔ never a distinct-string count; (c) the recorded mapping of
the owner's SEVEN spoken domains onto those eight labels, his *"goals, and goal
orientation"* mapping to BOTH. The count is FOURTEEN because the polish minted row 4b
(FAITH — the imposition) and did not re-run the parse. **Veto cost:** a shrink-only
registry walker seeded at thirteen passes green on a table missing a row, which is the
vacuity class this volume reds in code and had shipped in prose; and a single
"seven domains present" assertion cannot tell a dropped row from a dropped label from a
dropped owner-domain. ⭐ **The general rule this ruling installs: A TABLE EDIT AND ITS
COUNT ARE ONE EDIT** — every count in this volume is an EXECUTED SELF-ASSERTION, re-parsed
at the revision that states it. ⭐⭐ **AND THE RULE IS NOW ENFORCED RATHER THAN STATED
(J-HB-27): NO DENOMINATOR EDIT SHIPS WITHOUT A `countsweep.py` RUN, AND THE COUNT CENSUS
APPENDIX IS RE-PASTED AT EVERY FUTURE REVISION.**

**J-HB-24 — THE `sue_for_peace` JOIN IS LEARN-WHEN-KEYED, AND THE GATE IS NAMED.** The
episode is minted **IFF `bilateralOffer`** — MEASURED verbatim at HEAD `3e2bd309` in
`settlementStrategy.js#emitMove`, where the five key-bearing fields ride a spread
conditioned on exactly that predicate. A suit emitted on any other path mints NO episode,
takes NO credit, and carries its own explicit `NEEDS-A-CLOSE` row with a written
`closeOwed` (a general offer record, which nobody has signed). **Veto cost:** the flat
spelling "sue_for_peace is COVERED" reads as an instruction to stamp unconditionally, and
an implementer building that against a conditional emitter writes `treaty.habitEpisode`
from `undefined` values — a corrupt key on a persisted record, on the exact field Q1 is
asking the owner to approve. ⛔ **Q1's two-field widening is UNAFFECTED and stands as
declared:** the gate narrows WHEN the second field is written, never WHETHER the schema
carries it.

**J-HB-25 — DERIVE, DON'T ENUMERATE: THE ONE-TERM SET IS A REGISTRY QUERY.** The rows
that ship the ONE-TERM form are a QUERY against `habitForkRegistry.js`, not a sentence —
⭐ **and the query's predicate is corrected by J-HB-26 below.** The registry answers it; no
sentence in this volume does.
⚠ Worked examples are permitted and are labelled as such — EXECUTED at this revision, the
set is `{3, 6}`. **Veto cost:** the hand-list has now been wrong three times in three
directions inside one clause, and ⭐ **THE THREE ARE NAMED HERE IN THE SAME ORDER §3c NAMES
THEM, SO THE TWO HOMES OF THIS AUDIT TRAIL CANNOT DRIFT APART — reconciled at round four,
where they had:** (1) **the polish's** `{3, 6, 12}`, which carried site 12 — MONADIC and
DEFER, a member under no reading — and dropped sites 2 and 10; (2) **the attestation's
re-measurement** `{2, 3, 6}`, which recovered site 2 and still missed site 10; and (3)
**the attestation's own two-clause query** `{2, 3, 6, 10}`, which recovered site 10 and
carried two DEFER rows with it, and which **J-HB-26 corrects at round three** to `{3, 6}`.
**A restatement that disagrees with its source is worse than no restatement, because it is
the one an implementer reads.**

---

**THE TWO ROUND-THREE RULINGS (J-HB-26..J-HB-27), each vetoable. ⚠⚠ THE FIRST CORRECTS A
QUERY RATHER THAN AN ANSWER, AND THE SECOND IS THE INSTRUMENT THAT MAKES EVERY COUNT IN
THIS VOLUME EXECUTED RATHER THAN ASSERTED.**

**J-HB-26 — THE DERIVED-SET QUERY CARRIES A DISPOSITION FILTER: `LEARN` ∧ `dyadic` ∧
`anticipationWave === null`.** EXECUTED at this revision, that is **`{3, 6}`**.
**THE RATIONALE, which is the whole ruling:** the clause this set illustrates
characterizes **SHIPPED EXPRESSION FORMS** — which joined sites emit a habit load with one
multiplicand and no second term. **A DEFER row ships no expression**: no load point, no
`habitLoad` product, no line of code for a second multiplicand to be absent from. Sites 2
and 10 are DYADIC with a null wave and are DEFER (R18), so the two-clause query returned
them while the clause was asking about built code — **a true statement about registry
metadata and a false one about the build.** ⭐ **AND DEFER ROWS KEEP THEIR DECLARED ARITY,
WITH ONE SENTENCE SAYING WHAT IT IS FOR:** `arity` on a DEFER row is banked REGISTRY
METADATA recording the measured fact that a counterpart CAN bind at that seam, **and it
becomes load-bearing at PROMOTION — the moment HB-9 lands the row's close and the row
turns LEARN — so the promotion never has to re-open a measurement this volume already
executed.** ⚠ Site 12's exclusion is restated accurately here because the old spelling gave
the wrong reason: **it fails BOTH clauses — MONADIC (so not dyadic) AND DEFER (so it ships
nothing)** — where the volume previously excluded it on the arity clause alone.
**Veto cost:** dropping the filter puts DEFER rows back in a set that describes emitted
code, which is how site 10's row came to say it "ships the ONE-TERM form" while shipping
nothing at all — a sentence an implementer reads as a licence to build a load point at a
site with no close.

**J-HB-27 — EVERY COUNT IN THIS VOLUME IS DERIVED BY ONE PARSE INSTRUMENT, AND THE
INSTRUMENT SHIPS BESIDE THE VOLUME.** `countsweep.py` parses this document's OWN tables,
derives every counted quantity from them, and diffs each against a DECLARED list of the
prose homes that state it — quantity, parse recipe, home line-contents — reporting
PASS/FAIL per quantity. **THE ARGUMENT IS THE ROUND-THREE RECORD ITSELF.** J-HB-23
installed the rule *"A TABLE EDIT AND ITS COUNT ARE ONE EDIT"* and the rule was then
broken THREE more times in a single review round: the deferral book stayed at forty-two
after J-HB-24 added a coverage row; HB-3's charter stayed at seven grade maps after R18
enumerated close 8; and the STRATEGY checklist row stayed DYADIC after R17 refuted that
very word. ⚠⚠ **A RULE WITH NO INSTRUMENT IS A HOPE, AND THIS VOLUME HAD THREE ROUNDS OF
EVIDENCE FOR IT.** ⭐ **THE INSTRUMENT'S OWN LAW IS THE ONE IT ENFORCES — DERIVE, DON'T
RESTATE:** it holds no expected numbers of its own, only parse recipes and home phrases,
so a stale figure fails as a HOME DIFF naming both the phrase and what stands where the
figure should be. ⭐ **AND EVERY HOME ASSERTS ITS OWN OCCURRENCE COUNT, WHICH CLOSES THE
FIRST-MATCH DOCUMENT PIN CLASS** — a pin that finds its target by first match retargets
silently when a second match appears, so a home that occurs twice fails exactly as loudly
as a home that occurs zero times. **THE MALFORMED-ROW SCAN IS THE SAME INSTRUMENT'S
SECOND DOOR:** every table's data rows must carry its header's cell count, which is what
a struck row with a missing cell (§3d seam 4) had been evading. **Veto cost:** the
alternative is what this volume already tried three times — a stated rule, an honest
intention, and a fourth round that finds the fourth stale denominator. ⛔ **The one thing
that would make the instrument worthless is seeding it with expected values**; a walker
or a sweep carrying a transcribed number is a second denominator wearing a guard's
clothing, and it fails GREEN.

---

**THE ROUND-FOUR RULING (J-HB-28), vetoable. ⚠⚠ IT CLOSES THE CLASS J-HB-27 WAS
STRUCTURALLY BLIND TO, AND THE PROOF THAT THE CLASS IS REAL IS THAT THE INSTRUMENT FOUND
ITS OWN CHARTERING DEFECT ON ITS FIRST RUN.**

**J-HB-28 — A REFUTED PREMISE IS SCANNED FOR, NOT MERELY STRUCK.** `countsweep.py` gains a
SECOND FAMILY. Every premise in the refutation register R1-R18 declares SIGNATURE PHRASES
— the distinctive wording of the claim that fell — and the instrument scans the WHOLE
volume for each of them, REDDING on any occurrence that is not licensed.
⭐ **THE ARGUMENT, WHICH IS THE ROUND-FOUR RECORD ITSELF.** J-HB-27 closed the stale-NUMBER
class: a table grew and a sentence quoting its size did not. It is structurally incapable
of seeing the stale-CLAIM class, which is the same defect one level up — §0.3 kills a
premise at its source and a sentence somewhere else goes on asserting it, because nobody
re-read that page. ⚠⚠ **AND THE VOLUME HAD BEEN CARRYING EXACTLY THAT FOR THREE ROUNDS:**
J-HB-5 still said the episode key was one *"both sides compute independently"* long after
R4 was refuted and J-HB-13 ruled the key MINTED AT OPEN AND CARRIED. **A ruling built on a
premise its own register had killed is worse than a stale count**, because a count is
obviously checkable and a claim reads as authority.
**THE LICENCE IS POSITIVE, IN THREE KINDS, AND THAT SHAPE IS THE WHOLE DESIGN.** An
occurrence passes only by holding one of: **(a) REGISTER** — it lies inside §0.3, computed
from the document's own headings; **(b) STRUCK** — a strike token from a CLOSED set stands
within a BOUNDED RADIUS of it; **(c) QUOTED-TO-STRIKE** — it sits inside a quotation AND
the unit carries a quoting-to-strike label from a CLOSED set, both halves required,
because a quotation with no label is just an assertion wearing quotation marks.
⚠⚠ **THE RADIUS IS NOT DECORATION AND IT IS WHERE THIS COULD HAVE FAILED OPEN.** Licensing
on "a strike token appears somewhere nearby" is an enumeration on the CREDIT side — the
estate's own sharpest guard-vacuity class — and with no radius a 2,900-character block
that says ⛔ about one thing would license a stale premise restated about something else
2,000 characters later. MEASURED across every licensed occurrence in this volume the
largest true strike distance is **317 characters**; the radius sits just above it, and
§3a's occupation bullet — whose nearest ⛔ is **1,444** characters away — is licensed by
its QUOTATION instead, which is the tighter and more honest reason. A table ROW is its own
licensing unit for the same reason: a table is one contiguous run of lines, so scoring it
whole would let a ⛔ in one row absolve a different row.
⭐ **THE ANTI-VACUITY HALF, and it is the half that was got wrong once during this very
round.** A signature that has drifted out of the register matches nothing and passes
silently — the filename-anchored-pin class exactly. But "every signature must appear in
§0.3" is the WRONG rule, and writing it first is how the lesson was learned: **the
sharpest signatures are the ones that must appear NOWHERE**, because they are the DERIVED
restatement spellings a premise mutates into. ⛔ The ⛔ STRUCK phrase
*"both sides compute independently"* was never register wording — it was J-HB-5's
paraphrase of R4 — and its correct count is now ZERO. ⭐ **THAT THIS VERY SENTENCE HAD TO
BE MARKED IS THE DEMONSTRATION:** its first draft named the struck phrase bare while
explaining the scan, the scan REDDED its own charter, and the fix was to mark the
quotation rather than to widen the licence — which is the rule this ruling exists to
install, applied to itself on its first run.
So each signature declares an ANCHOR: **REGISTER-anchored** phrases must occur inside §0.3
or they are reported STALE (the typo/drift guard, and every premise must carry at least
one), while **FORBIDDEN** spellings are held to zero-or-licensed and a clean scan is
silence.
**Veto cost:** the volume goes back to enforcing its refutations by intention. §0.3 would
remain a register of things that are no longer true, with no instrument able to say
whether anything still asserts them — and the one instance already found proves the
honest answer is not "nothing". ⛔ **The same forbidden seeding applies here as to the
count family: this scan may hold search phrases and licences, and never an expected
number of hits**, because a scan that knows how many restatements to expect is a second
denominator wearing a guard's clothing.

---

## §7 OPEN CHAIR QUESTIONS (five, ranked by blocking power; each with a recommendation;
## all vetoable)

**Q1 — ⭐ RE-AIMED AT THE AMENDMENT, ⭐⭐ WIDENED AT THE POLISH FROM ONE FIELD TO TWO. Do
`deployment.habitEpisode` AND `treaty.habitEpisode` — TWO stamped fields on persisted
campaign records — carry the owner's signature, or do closes 1 and the sue arm wait?
(BLOCKS HB-3's close 1, therefore the flagship close, AND the `sue_for_peace` join the
chair ruled at P3.)**

⭐ **WHY IT WIDENED, said before the recommendation rather than after it.** P3 ruled that
where an existing grade already covers an action, the action JOINS rather than defers —
and the `sue_for_peace` arm is covered by close 2's treaty grades — ⚠ **CONDITIONALLY,
per J-HB-24: covered where `emitMove`'s `bilateralOffer` gate is true, closeless and
deferred where it is not.** But the chooser cannot
know the `treatyId` at the open, exactly as it cannot know `deployment.sinceTick`, so the
join needs the SAME carry road: a key minted at the open from three values the sue branch
already emits (`terms:<offererId>:<targetId>:<peaceFrontSinceTick>` — MEASURED present in
`emitMove`'s sue branch) and stamped onto the treaty record at mint. **That is a second
persisted-shape change and it is put in front of the owner as one.** ⛔ It would have been
easy to describe it as an implementation detail of a join the chair already ruled; it is
not, and the volume would rather be told no twice than smuggle the second field behind the
first.

**THE DISCHARGED HALF, recorded so nothing re-opens it.** The compile asked whether the
blanket sign-off covered a habit read at the war chooser alongside the signed spy read.
**A2 settles it: the war-chooser spy-before-decision wiring is SIGNED, and this volume
now treats it as released everywhere it appears** — §0.3 R1, §4 HB-5, §5 clause 5, §8.1
row 5, and §3d's seam 4′, which wires the belief-routed ANTICIPATION read at that same
block on the strength of it. The habit read itself rides the directive's own TOTAL SCOPE
clause, which postdates the grant and is broader than it. Two authorities, both
recorded, neither stretched, and no owner touch is owed for the reads.

**THE LIVE HALF.** H3 refuted the compile's claim that all seven closes can recompute
their key, and the chair's ruling is that keys are MINTED AT OPEN AND CARRIED. At close
1 that means ONE additional field on the persisted deployment record —
`deployment.habitEpisode`, copied at `warDeployment` step 4 from the `warIntentFor`
order the opener already holds, drop-when-absent, written only under
`habitConditioningEnabled`. **A persisted-shape change is an owner-gated class and this
volume does not decide it.** **RECOMMENDATION: SIGN IT, and the reasoning is that all
three alternatives are worse.** (i) MEASURED, `DeploymentRecord` is a deliberately
untyped bag (`@typedef {{ [k: string]: any }} DeploymentRecord`, with an in-source
rationale for the looseness), so no typedef, no schema version, and no migration moves —
absent-field records ARE the legacy shape and read as absent. ⭐ **AND THE SECOND RECORD
MEASURES THE SAME WAY — RE-EXECUTED BY SYMBOL AT HEAD `3e2bd309` FOR THIS ROUND, SO THE
OWNER PRICING A TWO-FIELD VETO SEES THE CHEAPNESS EVIDENCE FOR BOTH FIELDS AND INFERS
NEITHER FROM THE OTHER.** `TreatyRecord` is declared exactly once, in
`src/domain/worldPulse/peaceTermsCatalog.js`, as `/** @typedef {Record<string, unknown>}
TreatyRecord */` — an OPEN BAG over string keys; `peaceTerms.js`, `peaceTermsDocument.js`
and `peaceTermsOverlay.js` all `import(...).TreatyRecord` rather than re-declaring it, so
there is ONE declaration and no second shape to keep in step. Neither mint path validates
key shape: `mintTreaty` and `mintTreatyFromCarriedSheet` each return
`{ treaty, signingBeat, applyMintEffects, … }` with no allowlist and no exact-key check,
and a repo-wide search for a treaty key validator returns none. ⚠ **THE NEGATIVE CONTROL,
because "no validator" is the kind of absence that is easy to assert and hard to trust:**
the only key-shape assertions on a treaty record anywhere in the tree are NEGATIVE AND
SPECIFIC — `tests/domain/sovereigntyMarketStageWr10w.test.js` asserts
`expect(Object.keys(treaty)).not.toContain('victorId')` and the same for `loserId` and
`swapId` (the WR-10 drop-when-absent discipline) — and not one of them enumerates an
ALLOWED set, so an added `habitEpisode` key reds nothing that exists today. **Both fields
are the same cheap shape: one optional key on an open bag, absent-reads-as-absent, no
migration.** (ii) The alternative — an
HB-side index from `(attackerId, targetId)` to a pledge — is a SECOND index over a
relation the deployment record already holds, and second indexes over live relations are
where this estate's ghost-writes come from. (iii) Doing nothing means close 1 never
joins, which means the war chooser learns nothing, which means the program's anchor site
is decorative. ⚠ If the chair prefers to wait, the honest consequence is stated rather
than softened: **HB-3 ships with SEVEN closes and the war chooser reads a stock nothing
ever writes** — a dead arm at the one site the whole design is built around, and the
volume would rather be told no than ship that quietly. ⚠⚠ **THE DECLINE FIGURE IS SEVEN
AND IT IS RE-PARSED HERE AGAINST THE EIGHT-CLOSE TABLE; the ⛔ struck spelling was SIX,
and it was stale from the moment close 8 was enumerated.** A veto kills **close 1 ALONE**.
**Close 2 SURVIVES A VETO** — it rides BOTH ROADS, and its RECOMPUTED road
(`terms:<treatyId>`, site 8's accept/refuse episode) needs no stamped field at all; what a
veto of `treaty.habitEpisode` costs is the `sue_for_peace` JOIN, not the close. **So the
honest price of a decline is: seven of eight closes survive, `deploy` reads a stock
nothing writes, and the sue arm loses its keyed join** — a smaller price than "SIX", which
silently deleted a close that two roads reach and a close the estate already carries.

⚠⚠ **THE THIRD FIELD — DEFERRED AND VISIBLE, RULED AT ROUND FOUR RATHER THAN DECIDED.**
A THIRD spelling exists in this volume: **`occupationRecord.habitEpisode`**, in HB-9's
occupation-close bullet (§3a). **It is NOT part of this question and Q1 does not ask the
owner to sign it**, because HB-9's occupation close is DEFERRED — the field is a proposal
attached to a close nobody has minted, and asking for a signature on it now would be
asking the owner to approve a shape for a road that may never be built. **THE RULING: it
STAYS DEFERRED AND STAYS VISIBLE. If HB-9's occupation close converts to a carried road,
it carries a THIRD owner-gated persisted field and Q1 RE-OPENS AT THAT WAVE.** ⛔ The
persisted shape is an owner-gated class and this volume decides it neither here nor there.
⭐ **THE INSTRUMENT HOLDS THE DISTINCTION SO THIS SENTENCE DOES NOT HAVE TO BE TRUSTED:**
`foreign_fields_written` is scoped to §8.1's WAR seam row and derives
{`deployment.habitEpisode`, `treaty.habitEpisode`}; `foreign_fields_deferred` derives the
remainder and **asserts that every deferred spelling is named HERE** — so a fourth
spelling appearing anywhere in the volume reds this clause until somebody dispositions it,
and the deferred field can neither inflate Q1 nor quietly vanish from it.

**Q2 — Twelve circumstance classes: is `unpressed` the right total-fallback, and is the
precedence order right? (SHAPES EVERYTHING; blocks HB-0.)** Every habit in the world is
keyed on this vocabulary, and refinement 9 makes growth owner-signed — so the initial cut
is close to permanent. **RECOMMENDATION: accept the twelve and the declared precedence.**
The risk to weigh: `unpressed` will be the MODAL class in a quiet world, so most learning
in peacetime accrues under a single undifferentiated heading. That is arguably correct (a
court at ease has one manner) and arguably a wasted dimension. The alternative — dropping
`unpressed` and refusing to classify quiet decisions — costs totality, which is worth
more. **A measurement is owed at HB-0 regardless: the class-occupancy mix on generated
worlds, recorded in the header.** If `unpressed` exceeds ~70% of all classified decisions
the chair should reopen this question before HB-4 spends it.

**Q3 — The `Math.pow` residual: adopt the rounding fence now, or integer-domain decay?
(RISK CALL; blocks nothing.)** `decayTowardNeutral` uses `Math.pow(0.5, age/weeks)`,
which is implementation-approximated. The rounding fence (§3b) annihilates ULP
differences except on a measure-zero boundary set. A full cure — decay through a
precomputed rational half-life table over integer ages — would be exact.
**RECOMMENDATION: adopt the ROUNDING FENCE now and record integer-domain decay as a
future widening.** Reasons: the estate already carries this exposure at fifteen sites
including `dispositionLedger`, so habit adds no new class of risk; a rational table would
be a SECOND decay law in everything but name, which refinement 1 forbids; and the soak is
where a cross-device divergence would actually be observed. **The obligation this
recommendation takes on: the HB envelopes include a same-input cross-run stock-identity
check, so if the residual ever bites, the instrument sees it.**

**Q4 — Does HB earn the twelfth chartered coupling prefix, or ride an existing one?
(BLOCKS HB-2's first cross-layer row.)** `CHARTERED_VOLUME_PREFIXES` is ELEVEN at HEAD
and `COUPLING_ID_SHAPE` is BUILT from it, so the first `CPL-<n>.<DIR>.HB-<n>.<facet>` row
REDS until the prefix joins. **RECOMMENDATION: YES — add `HB` as the twelfth, with its
own `couplingRegistryHabit.js` leaf.** The habit substrate is read by WAR (the chooser),
TRADE (the contest), GRAMMAR (pact answers), POP (crowding), FAITH (traditions), INTERIOR
(seat decisions) and INFO (the anticipation half) — six ports reading one substrate, plus
INFO reading all of them. That is not a rider on someone else's volume. ⛔ **AND THE
COROLLARY, ruled here so it is not re-derived: NO eighth `LAYER_PATTERNS` family is
owed.** The habit SUBSTRATE leaves take ARGUED_UNLAYERED rows on the exact argument
already carrying four entries (`bandFamilies`, `bandedStock`, `lawWord`, `magicWorksAt`):
a shared vocabulary every layer spells against is not a port, and giving it a family
"would make every layer's reading of a band word a cross-layer coupling, which is hosting
by another name." The ANTICIPATION leaf joins INFO by NAME (`beliefDoctrineAnticipation.js`
matches the existing `/belief[A-Z]/` pattern) rather than by a new pattern.

**Q5 — Cross-class bleed: park it, or reserve a shape for it? (BLOCKS NOTHING; shapes the
future.)** Refinement 11 rules classes independent in v1. The design question the chair
may want to pre-answer: when bleed eventually arrives, is it a SIMILARITY MATRIX over
classes (12×12, owner-signed) or a HIERARCHY (`siege_pressed` inherits from `war_open`)?
**RECOMMENDATION: PARK IT, recorded, and reserve NOTHING.** A reserved shape is a shape
somebody builds toward, and the honest answer is that we will not know which is right
until the soak shows how sparse the per-class ledgers actually are. ⛔ **The one thing to
forbid now, in writing: bleed must never arrive as a tuning knob.** It is a structural
change with its own wave and its own owner signature, because a bleed coefficient is a
credit-assignment claim wearing a constant's clothing — exactly the creep refinement 4
exists to hold.

---

## §8 SEAMS PINNED BOTH SIDES

### 8.1 The THIRTEEN seam rows (⭐ row 13 added at the amendment — the campaign clock)

| # | Neighbour | The contract | The tripwire |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **SP-A substrate** (`bandedStock`, `bandFamilies`) | HB is the FIRST `src` rider of `decayTowardNeutral` and the FIRST consumer of `SEVERITY_LADDER`. It picks a `HALF_LIFE_BANDS` member; it never authors a rate. It reads `SEVERITY_LADDER` rungs; it never authors a scale. Half two of `spBandFamilies.walker` already enforces the singularity — no module but the family's own may speak a severity rung | a second `Math.pow(0.5, …)` anywhere in the HB module set REDS the decay-singularity scan; a habit-local severity spelling REDS the existing walker |
| 2 | **SP-B belief axes** | `doctrineBands` is a FOURTH subject family through the five joins — one by-name gate line, one derivation behind THE ONE TRUTH DOOR, one droppable arm, one `@property`, one `SUBJECT_AXIS_FIELDS` member. J-SP-1 binds: no second fold, no second decay law, no parallel believed-doctrine ledger | the SP axis-vocabulary walker extended to import BOTH sides and assert equality in BOTH directions; a doctrine token spelled outside the axis leaf REDS |
| 3 | **ES (the tap ladder + plants)** | `doctrine` joins the ES `legRefs` closed set as a SEVENTH member — an ES-VOLUME vocabulary amendment landed in HB-8's own commit, never smuggled. Products ride `reconcileBelief` as synthetic reports (J-ES-5). Tap depth maps to sheet fidelity via `TAP_DEPTH`, never the array index. ⚠ The whole arm is UNREACHABLE until SP-D lands `errandSpineEnabled` | the ES mint's six-member refusal test gains a seventh row and REDS if the sets disagree; the `applyBeliefOverrides` writer-boundary scan; the asset-less-delta anchored negative |
| 4 | **The plant road** (`disinformationPlant`, `brokerageServicesPlant`) | Planted doctrine rides the REPORT road with a `plant:*` disinfo lineage row. ⛔ The OVERRIDE road is NAMED AND REFUSED: `PAID_PLANT_OVERRIDE_KEYS` is an exact-six-key equality and a seventh field is a SILENT DROP. ### 8.2 THE TUNING SEAM

Every value in `HABIT_TUNING` is a RAW-AUTHORED PROPOSAL. **NOTHING enters
`proposedSoakBands.js`, NO `SOAK_COUPLINGS` member is added, and
`SOAK_BAND_MANIFEST_VERSION` does NOT move — until the owner signs at the soak redo.**
That signature is the same act that admits the bands to the manifest, and it is
constitutional: THE PROMISE makes the tuning signature a specific, versioned act.

⚠ A habit band would require a SEVENTH `SOAK_COUPLINGS` member (the vocabulary is closed
at six), which is a manifest-SHAPE change and therefore a version bump 2 → 3 — an
owner-signature-surface change, not a data edit. That cost is stated now so it is priced
at the soak rather than discovered there.

⚠ Every habit dial lives in the seeded generation/worldPulse pipeline and is therefore
**golden-shifting and LANE-B FOREVER** on `autoTunableRegistry`'s denylist: the auto-tune
loop may PROPOSE a retune from a band divergence; it may NEVER auto-apply one.

AGAINST TUNING-BEARING WAVES ONLY (P8 / J-HB-21).** ⚠ **THE AMENDMENT'S SPELLING ("EQUAL
verbatim to its §7 rows", stated here and at §3b) IS STRUCK AS INCOHERENT:** §7 is the

DESIGN` note naming what they author instead.** ⚠ The chair's ruling estimated nine

### 8.4 ⭐⭐ THE EPOCH JOINT LAW — stated identically in both volumes

> **HABITS ARE DERIVED STATE OVER LIVED HISTORY. THE EPOCH IS THE ADDRESS OF THE FUTURE
> LIVED FROM A POINT. Same lived history ⇒ same habits, always. A re-drawn future
> re-derives habits from the NEW history deterministically, because backtrack DISCARDS a
> suffix of history rather than editing it, and habits ride the discarded world.**

Four mechanisms make it true rather than merely compatible, each measured:

1. **THE LIVED-PAST LAW.** A committed `pulseHistory` entry is NEVER rewritten; backtrack
   DISCARDS a suffix. The branching model is LINEAR-WITH-DISCARD, and the discarded
   timeline is not retained, not diffable, not restorable.
2. **UNDO IS NOT A SPLICE.** `capturePulseSnapshot` does `cloneJson(worldState)` and
   `restorePulseSnapshotOnDraft` puts the whole thing back. **Habits stamped by an undone
   advance VANISH WITH THE WORLD.** The ONLY way a credit is lost is by being un-lived,
   which is precisely correct.
3. **THE RE-ADVANCE IS A GENUINELY NEW FUTURE.** The restored world carries the previous
   tick's stamp; the re-advance composes `tick + 1`; the current-tick selection rule
   refuses the stale stamp; the writer stamps the NEWLY MINTED epoch; every family-1 draw
   keys off a value that has never existed before. The epoch volume's own words: *"A DM
   who backtracks on vibes and re-advances gets a different future, and the ledger is
   where the difference enters the stream."*
4. ⛔ **HABIT VALUES NEVER ENTER A STREAM ADDRESS.** The epoch is an entropy re-root; a
   habit stock is a WEIGHT INPUT. Keeping them in separate sub-keys is what preserves
   "same history ⇒ same habits" under a re-drawn future: the re-advance draws differently
   because the EPOCH moved, and the habits it starts from are the ones the restored world
   carried. **If a habit value ever fed a stream key, the two would be mutually recursive
   across a backtrack** and neither volume's claim would survive.

**THREE AGREEMENTS THE TWO VOLUMES RECORD IDENTICALLY:**

- **THE COLLAPSE PATH.** `collapseIntervalHistory` composes by SPREAD, so mid-interval
  habit credits survive the N→1 record collapse — CONFIRMED at HEAD. ⚠ Do NOT inherit the
  epoch volume's revision-5 spelling ("`spatialLedgers` is neither read nor written on
  that path"): it is FALSIFIABLE IN ONE GREP, because three accessor calls sit on that
  path for the provenance reconcile. ⚠ **CORRECTED (M4): this volume carries the
  corrected sentence and says nothing about what the EP draft carries** — that is
  another session's document, and a read-only volume cannot make an assertion about
  it true.
- **THE DECLARED NON-INVARIANT.** Ledger rows ≠ `pulseHistory` length, BY DESIGN, in both
  volumes and for the same reason: a multi-tick advance leaves many entries behind one
  surviving record. Both state it at three homes because it is the divergence most likely
  to be re-found and mis-diagnosed.
- ⭐⭐ **THE PREVIEW LAW — ⚠ CORRECTED AT THE AMENDMENT ON BOTH ITS PROVENANCE AND ITS
  RECEIPT (M4 + L1).**
  **(a) PROVENANCE — HB DEFERS.** The compile presented the preview seam as "NOT stated
  in either volume today". That is wrong: **the EPOCH draft already carries the measured
  premise, its ruling (INHERIT, NEVER MINT), its pin and its mutant.** ⛔ **THE PREVIEW
  PREMISE IS THE EPOCH VOLUME'S AND THIS VOLUME CROSS-REFERENCES IT RATHER THAN
  RESTATING IT** — restating a sibling's ruling is how two documents start disagreeing.
  This volume also makes NO assertion about what the EP draft "now carries": it is
  another session's document and a read-only volume has no power to make such a sentence
  true. What is genuinely HB's, and all that is claimed here, is the **CREDIT-NEVER
  COROLLARY** below.
  **(b) THE RECEIPT — the printed one is FALSE and is replaced.** The compile offered
  "an executed grep for `commit ?|if (commit)|commit &&|!commit` over `pulseKernel.js`
  exits 1". **EXECUTED AT HEAD: it exits 0**, matching `if (commitments.changed)
  memoryState = (commitments.worldState);` — the alternative `if \(commit` matches the
  prefix of `commitments`. ⚠ **A grep receipt whose pattern can match a longer identifier
  is not evidence of an absence; it is evidence nobody ran it.** THE HONEST RECEIPT is a
  word-boundary scan and an ENUMERATION rather than an exit code: `grep -nE
  "(^|[^a-zA-Z])commit([^a-zA-Z]|$)" src/domain/worldPulse/pulseKernel.js` yields
  **exactly five kinds of hit and no sixth** — the JSDoc `@param {boolean} [args.commit]`,
  the destructure `commit = false` in `simulateCampaignWorldPulse`, three prose comments,
  and `committed: commit`. **`commit` is read as a LABEL and never as a CONDITION.** The
  substantive claim survives intact; only the proof is replaced.
  **(c) HB's OWN COROLLARY, which is the new thing.** Because preview and advance are
  behaviourally identical, **a preview MUST READ habits and MUST NEVER CREDIT them.** A
  forecast that grades outcomes teaches the actors from a future that was never lived,
  arbitrarily many times, at the one seam a user can re-run at will — and **no existing
  pin would see it.** HB-2 lands the anchored negative: a preview run leaves the habit
  sub-ledger byte-identical, asserted with a real pledge and a real close inside the
  previewed tick, never on an empty harness.
- **THE REPLAY HORIZON.** Habit replay reconstructed from recorded history is bounded at
  `MAX_HISTORY = 80` advances exactly as epoch replay is — a declared deferral, not a
  discovery, stated in-file.

⚠ **SELF-INVALIDATION DOES NOT TRANSFER.** The epoch's `latest` stamp needs no lifecycle
exception because any path that drops, rewinds, or outruns it makes it UNSELECTABLE.
**Habits get no such gift** — they are MEANT to persist across ticks — so HB owes every
lifecycle row explicitly (§8.5) where EP could lean on the property.

### 8.6 ⭐ THE PROMISE — learning-as-state does not breach it

THE PROMISE, ratified and constitutional: **a seed is a world, forever.** Tuning changes
are owner-SIGNED and versioned; nothing re-rolls behind the DM's back; there is no
wall-clock input to any stream.

**The argument, in full, that habit conditioning leaves it intact:**

**(a) THE MAPPING STAYS A FUNCTION.** THE PROMISE is the claim that
`(seed, code, DM acts) → world` is a FUNCTION — the same inputs give the same world,
always. Habit adds a term to that function's body. It does not add an argument, and it
does not make the function stateful across invocations: the habit ledger is derived
*inside* the computation, from the same history the computation itself produces. A world
replayed from its seed re-derives every habit stock, in order, to the same integer.

**(b) NOTHING NEW IS NON-DETERMINISTIC.** The habit path takes ZERO draws — it consumes
no PRNG stream and creates none. It reads no wall clock: ⭐ **ages are the difference of
two integers read off `worldState.calendar.elapsedWeeks`, the authoritative campaign
clock — never `Date`, never a tick, and NEVER a conversion** (R10 / J-HB-17).
⚠ **CORRECTED AT THE POLISH (P6), AND THIS ONE MATTERED MORE THAN A SPELLING:** the
amendment's own sentence here read *"ages are TICK deltas converted through
`INTERVAL_WEEKS`"* — which describes exactly the thing §8.1 row 13 forbids by source
scan (*"⛔ HB mints NO tick→week converter, imports `INTERVAL_WEEKS` NOWHERE, and stamps
no ticks | a source scan asserting no HB module imports `intervalWeeks.js`"*). **THE
PROMISE's own determinism paragraph was grounded on a seam this volume's own tripwire
reds.** A wave brief that built §8.6(b)'s sentence would have shipped a module the
seam-13 walker fails on day one. Its only
floating-point operation is the shared `decayTowardNeutral`, and its output is rounded to
integer units before any comparison (§3b, the ROUNDING FENCE), which is a strictly
stronger discipline than the fifteen existing sites that compute the same power.

**(c) THE STREAM IS UNTOUCHED.** MEASURED, not argued: `stableSampleByWeight` takes
exactly one `rng.random()` regardless of the weights handed to it; keyed races compute
`weight * hash01(key)` with a per-candidate key independent of the weight. **A habit tilt
changes which candidate a fixed draw selects. It never changes how many draws are taken
or what any draw returns.** This is why the four flags can light without re-seeding
anything, and why a dark world is byte-identical rather than approximately identical.

**(d) THE ONE SHIFT IS DISCLOSED, SIGNED, AND VERSIONED — which is exactly what THE
PROMISE PROVIDES FOR.** Lighting `habitConditioningEnabled` changes the mapping from seed
to world ONCE, for worlds run after the signature. That is a TUNING ACT of the class THE
PROMISE already governs: owner-signed, versioned per release, disclosed as a same-seed
shift, never silent, never a re-record without a ruling. Existing campaigns are unaffected
until the flag lights in their rules, and an existing campaign with an absent ledger reads
a factor of exactly 1.0 — **byte-identity for legacy campaigns is a property of the
neutral-identity door, not of a compatibility shim.**

**(e) THE STRONGEST FORM OF THE CLAIM, and it is worth stating because it is
counterintuitive.** Habit does not make worlds LESS reproducible; it makes them MORE
*legible*. Before habit, "why did this court deploy again?" had no answer beyond a score.
After habit, it has a named answer over a closed vocabulary, replayable to the tick, and
readable in the dossier. **THE PROMISE is a promise about determinism, and this program
spends none of it.** What it spends is neutrality — worlds after the signature differ
from worlds before it — and that is the currency every tuning act spends, under the
owner's signature, by design.

and eighteen times now, it was.*

**HOW TO READ A FAILURE.** A `FAIL` is never "the script is out of date". It is exactly one
of two things: a table was edited and a sentence was not, or a sentence was edited and the
table was not. Both are the same bug and both are fixed by re-parsing — never by editing
the script's EXPECTATIONS. ⛔ **A sweep seeded with expected values fails GREEN**, which is
the vacuity class this volume reds throughout, so the instrument is forbidden to carry one.
⭐ **ROUND FOUR SCOPED THAT RULE RATHER THAN LOOSENING IT:** it binds on EXPECTATIONS —
transcribed quantities, expected sets, figures the script would compare the volume against
— and NOT on the numeral→word RENDERING TABLE, whose growth can never change a verdict.
The `HOMES` column counts prose homes diffed; `XCHK` counts STRUCTURAL cross-checks —
assertions between two of the volume's own tables, which is how a quantity with no prose
home (the coverage table, the arity table, the disposition partition) is still held.

**THE INSTRUMENT WAS PROVEN BY MUTANT, BECAUSE A GUARD THAT CANNOT BE REDDENED CANNOT BE
PROVEN. THE ROUND-FOUR BATTERY IS FIFTEEN, ALL FIFTEEN BEHAVED, AND EVERY ONE ASSERTED THE
FILE ACTUALLY CHANGED BEFORE THE RUN.** The round-three seven re-ran unchanged against the
rebuilt instrument — a stale deferral-book denominator reds `deferral_book`; a stale
grade-map count reds `closes`; a stale derived set reds `derived_one_term_set`; a reverted
`per-action` arity reds `site7_arity`; an added checklist row reds `checklist_rows`,
`checklist_labels` and `checklist_decomposition` together; a row with a cell removed reds
`malformed_row_scan`; a DUPLICATED home phrase reds `waves` on its occurrence count, which
is the assertion that closes the FIRST-MATCH DOCUMENT PIN class. **EIGHT ARE NEW.** ⭐ F3's
exact escapee: re-spelling the TREATY record's stamped-field owner to a different record
name INSIDE THE WAR SEAM ROW ONLY — which passed at round three because only the
CARDINALITY was checked, and two is two whatever the two are called — now reds
`foreign_fields_written`, because the home phrase is BUILT from the derived names and a
second home (J-HB-13) is diffed against it as a SET. ⚠ **The mutant's re-spelling is
described here rather than written out, and that is itself a round-four finding:** the
first draft of this paragraph spelled it, and `foreign_fields_deferred` — which scans the
WHOLE volume for `<record>.habitEpisode` — correctly reported a THIRD deferred field that
existed only in a sentence about a mutant. **A census appendix must not mint the spellings
its own census counts.** ⭐ F2's two crashes: an added
coverage row (43 → 44) and an added judgment block now produce a FULL census that fails the
right quantity, and a third mutant drives the book to **47** — a number the rendering table
has no word for — to prove the numeral fallback rather than merely the enlarged table.
⭐ And the four that prove J-HB-28: an unmarked re-plant at a fresh home REDS; a
legitimately quoted-and-labelled one stays GREEN; a drifted register wording reds as a
STALE SIGNATURE; and a premise planted with a strike token deliberately pushed BEYOND the
licensing radius REDS — which is the mutant that proves the radius is load-bearing and not
decoration, because without it the licence would fail open on any long block.

⚠⚠ **THE BATTERY FOUND A DEFECT OF ITS OWN, AND IT IS THE SAME DEFECT F2 NAMED, ONE DOOR
OVER.** The round-three record said a row with a cell removed reds `malformed_row_scan`. Re-run
at round four against the COVERAGE table, it did not: `coverage()` indexed `c[3]` on a
three-cell row and the instrument died of an `IndexError` **before the malformed-row scan
ever ran**, printing nothing at all. **An instrument that aborts on the edit it exists to
catch has a silence a reader will read as "nothing found".** Three cures landed together:
short rows are PADDED so every parser is total (the raw row is what the scan measures, so
the finding is preserved exactly); the two parser `raise`s that could kill a census over one
bad cell now record a per-quantity note instead; and a BACKSTOP wraps the whole derivation so
that whatever a future edit does, the instrument's last act is to PRINT a named PARSE ABORT
rather than a traceback. ⭐ **The generalizable rule, and it is the round-four lesson:
EVERY guard must be run against the edit it was built to catch, not merely against a
plausible-looking mutation** — the round-three claim was true of the table it was tried on
and false of the next one.

**WHAT THE SWEEP CAUGHT, recorded because an instrument's first catch is its warrant.**
At round three, beyond the findings it was chartered to close, it found TWO stale
denominators nobody had reported: the judgment-block census read twenty-five while §6 held
more, and a third `<record>.habitEpisode` spelling — `occupationRecord.habitEpisode` —
living in HB-9's occupation-close bullet. That third spelling is now a derived quantity
(`foreign_fields_deferred`) **and Q1 is required to name it**: the deferred field can
neither inflate Q1 nor vanish from it, and a FOURTH spelling appearing anywhere reds until
somebody dispositions it. ⭐ **AT ROUND FOUR THE STRUCK-PREMISE FAMILY MADE ITS OWN FIRST
CATCH, WHICH IS THE ONE THAT MATTERS MOST:** J-HB-5 — a RULING, not a count — had been
resting on *"an episode key both sides compute independently"* since the compile, three
rounds after §0.3 refuted it and J-HB-13 replaced it. **A stale ruling reads as authority
in a way a stale number never does**, and nothing in this volume could see it until the
scan existed.
